On Provability Logic of HA
Mojtaba Mojtahedi

Abstract
We axiomatize the provability logic of HA and show that it is decidable. Moreover we axiomatize the preservativity and relative admissibility for several modal logics extending 𝗂𝖪𝟦 {\sf iK4} . As a main tool, we also provide some sort of semantics, called provability semantics, for modal logics extending 𝗂𝖦𝖫 {\sf iGL} , which is a mixture of usual Kripke semantics and provability in propositional modal logics.



1
Introduction

Provability logic is a propositional modal logic
with the following intended meaning for
\Box A
: “
A
is
provable in some formal theory
{\sf T}
”.
The first such
explicit interpretation for modal operator
is
[
Gödel, 1933
]
, where it uses
provability interpretation for the
modal operator
\Box
in classical modal logic
{\sf S4}

for embedding of intuitionistic propositional logic
{\sf IPC}

in
{\sf S4}
.
This result together with Gödel’s arithmetization of syntax/proof
for the aim of incompleteness theorems

[
Gödel, 1931
]
, are start points to the field of provability logic.
Since then in this field of study,
many interesting researches have been done
and still many problems remains open.
We refer the reader to
[
Beklemishev and Visser, 2006
;
Artemov and Beklemishev, 2004
]

for surveys on them.
A celebrated result in provability logic,
is the characterization of provability logic of Peano Arithmetic

PA

[
Solovay, 1976
;
Löb, 1955
]
. More precisely,

[
Solovay, 1976
;
Löb, 1955
]
prove that
\hbox{\sf GL}{}\vdash A
iff
for every arithmetical
PA
-interpretation
\alpha_{{}_{\sf PA}}

we have
\hbox{\sf PA}{}\vdash\alpha_{{}_{\sf PA}}(A)
, in which

GL
is the Gödel-Löb logic defined as
{\sf K4}
plus the Löb’s principle
\Box(\Box A\to A)\to\Box A
. Also
\alpha_{{}_{\sf PA}}

is called
PA
-interpretation if the following conditions met:

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf PA}}(a)
is an arbitrary first-order
sentence in the language of arithmetic for every atomic
a
.

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf PA}}
commutes with boolean connectives

\vee
,
\wedge
and
\to
.

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf PA}}(\Box A)
is an arithmetization
(formalization in the first-order language of arithmetic)
for: “
\alpha_{{}_{\sf PA}}(A)
is provable in
PA
”.

For more details see
[
Smoryński, 1985
;
Boolos, 1995
]
.
It is well-known that the mentioned result is robust,
i.e. it can be generalized
to other strong-enough first-order
classical
theories
like
{\sf I\Delta_{0}}+{\sf exp}
,
{\sf ZF}
and
{\sf ZFC}
.

There is another interesting road in provability logic, which considers provability interpretations in Heyting’s Arithmetic,
the intuitionistic fragment of
PA
.
First results in this field goes back to

[
Myhill, 1973
;
Friedman, 1975
]
, where they prove that

\Box(B\vee C)\to(\Box B\vee\Box C)
does not belong to the
provability logic of
HA
. Nevertheless,

[
Leivant, 1979
]
shows that
the axiom schema
\Box(B\vee C)\to\Box(\Box B\vee C)

belongs to the provability logic of
HA
. Then

[
Visser, 1981
;
Visser, 1982
]
studies
the provability logic of
HA
and characterizes its
letterless fragment.
[
Visser, 2002
;
Iemhoff, 2003
;
Iemhoff, 2001c
]

consider a generalization of provability, called preservativity,
which is intuitionistic version of its akin, interpretability

[
Visser, 1998
;
Visser, 1990
]
.
The propositional language for preservativity,
named
\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
in this paper, has a
binary modal operator
\rhd
with following interpretation
for
A\rhd B
:

For every
\Sigma_{1}
-sentence
S
if
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash S\to A
then

\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash S\to B
.

Albert Visser axiomatizes a logic, called
{\sf{iPH}}
and together with
Dick de Jongh proves the soundness of
{\sf{iPH}}
for above-mentioned arithmetical interpretations.
Then Rosalie Iemhoff conjectures that
{\sf{iPH}}
is also complete for the arithmetical interpretations.
Although results of this paper reinforce Iemhoff’s conjecture,
the arithmetical completeness of
{\sf{iPH}}
remains open.

[
Ardeshir and Mojtahedi, 2018
;
Visser and Zoethout, 2019
;
Ardeshir and Mojtahedi, 2019
]
characterize
the provability logic of
HA
and
\hbox{\sf HA}{}^{*}
(a self completion of
HA
introduced in
[
Visser, 1982
]
) for
\Sigma_{1}
-substitutions.
In some sense,
[
Mojtahedi, 2022b
]
shows that
the provability logic of
HA
for
\Sigma_{1}
-substitutions,
named
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}
here, is essentially
{\sf iGLC_{a}}
. More precisely,

[
Mojtahedi, 2022b
]
defines some translation
(.)^{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(-0.2,0.4){{ {\sf h}}}\end{picture}\!}
which embeds

{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}
into
{\sf iGLC_{a}}
.
[
Mojtahedi, 2021
]
characterizes

\Sigma_{1}
-provability logics of
HA
and
\hbox{\sf HA}{}^{*}
relative in

PA
and the standard model
\mathbb{N}
.

In the current paper we axiomatize the provability logic of

HA
as
{\sf iGL}
plus
\Box A\to\Box B
for every

A
and
B
with following property
(annotated
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf iGL}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ B
in this paper):

For every
E\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
, if
{\sf iGL}\vdash E\to A
then
{\sf iGL}\vdash E\to B
.

The precise
definition of
{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
is quite technical and might be found in

section

2.8
. Roughly speaking,

{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
is the set of all propositions which are
projective relative in
{\sf NNIL}
and are self-complete.

Road map

Section

2
includes all elementary and general definitions
and their related facts. We also included two main required results
in
section

2.14
.
Then in
section

3
we elevate the results of
[
Mojtahedi, 2022a
]

and axiomatize
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf iGL}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \
together with several other preservativity/admissibility relationships.
In
section

4
we provide
a Kripke-style semantic, called provability semantic, for
which we have soundness and completeness of
the provability logic of
HA
.
Finally in
section

5
we use provability semantics to reduce
arithmetical completeness to its
\Sigma_{1}
-version, in a manner like we had in
[
Ardeshir and Mojtahedi, 2015
]
.

2
Preliminary definitions and facts

This section is mainly
devoted to elementary definitions and facts.
First we
define propositional languages
(
section

2.1
) and substitutions (
sections

2.2
and
2.3
).
Then we define several axiom schemata and propositional logics (
section

2.4
).

Section

2.5
defines two complexity measures
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)

and
c^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
.
Kripke semantic for the intuitionistic modal logics is defined
in
section

2.6
. To be self contained, we also proved Kripke completeness of
{\sf iGL}
and
{\sf iGLC_{a}}
in
section

2.6
.

Section

2.7
defines the Gödel’s translation
[
Gödel, 1933
]
.
Some notations on sets of propositions are defined in
section

2.8
.

Section

2.9
defines the set
{\sf NNIL}

of propositions and then states some properties of them.

Section

2.10
defines relative admissibility and preservativity
and proves some elementary facts about them.

Section

2.11
introduces the greatest lower bound relative in some
set
\Gamma
of propositions and proves some of its elementary properties. In
section

2.13
we prove simultaneous fixed-point theorem for
{\sf iGL}
. Finally in
section

2.14
we state two salient results from
[
Ardeshir and Mojtahedi, 2018
;
Mojtahedi, 2022a
]
which are crucial for the characterization of the provability logic of
HA
.

2.1
Propositional language

The non-modal propositional language
\mathcal{L}_{0}
includes connectives
\vee
,
\wedge
,
\to
,
\bot
and
countably infinite atomic variables
{\sf var}:=\{x_{1},x_{2},\ldots\}

and also countably infinite atomic parameters

{\sf par}:=\{p_{1},p_{2},\ldots\}
.
The presence of parameters in the propositional language is
quite technical: As we will see later it is better to have some
atomics in the propositional language with the intended meaning of

\Sigma_{1}
-sentences. So in the axiomatizations
which will be defined in
section

2.4
we always
have this intended meaning in mind and thus we have
the axiom
p_{i}\to\Box p_{i}
in
{\sf iK4}
. There will be one more consideration regarding this intended meaning for parameters:
We can not substitute parameters arbitrarily. The only permitted
substitution for a parameter is such that it does not violate
the intended
\Sigma_{1}
-interpretation for it.
Negation
\neg
is defined
as
\neg A:=A\to\bot
and
\top:=\bot\to\bot
.
The union
{\sf var}\cup{\sf par}
is
annotated as
{\sf atom}
, the set of atomics. Also
define

{\sf B}:=\{\Box B:B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}\}\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf parb}:={\sf par}\cup{\sf B}\cup\{\bot\}\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf atomb}:={\sf parb}\cup{\sf var}.

We use the notation
\mathcal{L}_{0}(X)
for the set of all boolean combinations of propositions in
X
, i.e.
\mathcal{L}_{0}(X)
is the minimum set including
X\cup\{\bot\}
which is closed under
conjunction, disjunction and implication.
The modal language
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
is defined
as
\mathcal{L}_{0}
plus the modal unary operator
\Box
.
Also
\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
indicates the propositional language
\mathcal{L}_{0}
augmented with a binary modal operator
\rhd
.
Whenever we consider the language
\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
, we assume
that
\Box B:=\top\rhd B
.
Hence in this sense,

\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
is an extension of
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
.

2.2
Propositional substitutions

A (propositional) substitution
\theta
is a
function on propositional language
which commutes with all connectives. More precisely

\theta
satisfies the following conditions:

•

\theta(a)
is a proposition in the language
\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}

for every
a\in{\sf atom}
.

•

\theta(B\circ C)=\theta(B)\circ\theta(C)
for every
\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge,\to\}
.

•

\theta(\bot)=\bot
.

•

\theta(B\rhd C):=\theta(B)\rhd\theta(C)
.

By default we assume that all substitutions are identity over

{\sf par}
.
However at some places we need to substitute parameters as well, and of course we will make those places clear to the reader.
For a substitution
\theta
, the function
\hat{\theta}
is defined same as
\theta
except for boxed propositions for which
\hat{\theta}
operates as identity:

•

\hat{\theta}{(B\circ C)}=\hat{\theta}(B)\circ\hat{\theta}(C)
for every
\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge,\to\}
.

•

\hat{\theta}(\bot)=\bot
.

•

\hat{\theta}(B\rhd C):=B\rhd C
.

We call
\hat{\theta}

outer
substitution. Hence
\hat{\theta}{}
and
\theta

are the same in the case of non-modal language.

2.3
Arithmetical substitutions

An arithmetical substitution is a function
\alpha
on
set of atomic variables and parameters

{\sf atom}
such that
\alpha(a)

is a first-order arithmetical sentence
for every
a\in{\sf atom}
and
\alpha(a)\in\Sigma_{1}

for every
a\in{\sf par}
. Moreover
\alpha
is called
a
\Sigma_{1}
-substitution if
\alpha(a)\in\Sigma_{1}
for every

a\in{\sf atom}
.

An arithmetical substitution
\alpha
may be extended
to
\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
as follows:

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(a):=\alpha(a)
for every
a\in{\sf atom}
, and
\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(\bot)=\bot
.

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}
commutes with boolean connectives:

\vee,\wedge
and
\to
.

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(A\rhd B)
is defined as an arithmetization of following statement:

For every
E\in\Sigma_{1}
, if
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash E\to\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(A)

then
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash E\to\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(B)
.

Note that above definition for
\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}
is compatible with the
well-known provability interpretation for
\Box
when
one assume
\Box B:=\top\rhd B
.

A strong variant
\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}
is defined similarly:

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}(a):=\alpha(a)
for every
a\in{\sf atom}
, and
\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}(\bot)=\bot
.

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}
commutes with boolean connectives:

\vee,\wedge
and
\to
.

•

\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}(A\rhd B)
is defined as
\varphi
in conjunction to its provability statement in
HA
, i.e.
\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}(A\rhd B):=\varphi\wedge\Box_{{}_{\sf HA}}\varphi
, in which
\varphi
is
an arithmetization of following statement:

“for every
E\in\Sigma_{1}
, if
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash E\to\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}(A)

then
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash E\to\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}(B)
.”

2.4
Propositional logics

We consider
{\sf IPC}
as the intuitionistic propositional logic over
the modal language
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
,
i.e. a set of propositions in
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
which is closed under modus ponens (

A

A\to B

B

) and includes all of the following axiom-schemata:

•

A\to(B\to A)
,

•

(A\to(B\to C))\to((A\to B)\to(A\to C))
,

•

A\to(B\to(A\wedge B))
,

•

(A\wedge B)\to A
,
(A\wedge B)\to B
,

•

(A\to C)\to((B\to C)\to((A\vee B)\to C))
,

•

A\to(A\vee B)
,
B\to(A\vee B)
.

By default, we use
\vdash
for
derivability in
{\sf IPC}
.
The following axiom-schemata are defined:

K
:

\Box(A\to B)\to(\Box A\to\Box B)
.

4
:

\Box A\to\Box\Box A
.

L
:

\Box(\Box A\to A)\to\Box A
. (The
Löb
’s axiom)

\underline{\sf C_{p}}
:

p\to\Box p
for every
p\in{\sf par}
.

\underline{\sf C_{a}}
:

a\to\Box a
for every
a\in{\sf atom}
.

\underline{{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})}
:

\Box A\to\Box B

for every
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
, in which

\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
is defined in
section

2.10
.

{\underline{\sf H}}
:

\underline{{\sf H}({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}},{{\sf iGL}})}
in which

{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
is as defined in
section

2.8
.

\underline{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}
:

\underline{{\sf H}({{\sf S}{\sf N}},{{\sf iGLC_{a}}})}
in which

{\sf S}{\sf N}
is as defined in
section

2.8
.

\underline{{\sf H}^{\Box}}
:

\underline{{\sf H}({{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}},{{\sf iGL}})}
in which

{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
is as defined in
section

2.8
.

For an axiom schema
\underline{\sf X}
, let
\overline{\sf X}

indicate
\Box\underline{\sf X}
and also
{\sf X}
indicate

\underline{\sf X}\wedge\overline{\sf X}
.
Given a logic
{\sf L}
and axiom-schemata
{\sf X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}}
,
the logic
{\sf LX_{1}\ldots X_{n}}
is defined as
{\sf L}
plus the axioms
{\sf X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}}
.
Then we define following modal logics:

•

i
:
{\sf IPC}
plus the box of all axioms in
{\sf IPC}
plus

{\sf C_{p}}
.

•

{\sf iGL}:={\sf iK4}{\sf L}
. (A sequent calculus for
{\sf iGL}

is provided in
[
van der Giessen and Iemhoff, 2021
]
)

Note that in this setting,
{\sf iGL}
and
{\sf iK4}
are closed under necessitation:

A

\Box A

, and moreover they include the
axiom-schema
{\sf C_{p}}
. It is not difficult to observe that these
extensions of standard
{\sf iGL}
and
{\sf iK4}
are equivalent to their
standard counterpart, as far as we are working with the standard
non-parametric language.

2.5
Complexity measures
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
and
c^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)

Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
define

c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
as the maximum number of nested implications which are outside boxes and

c^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
is defined as
maximum of
the number of parameters and boxed subformulas in
A

and
\max\{c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B):\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)\}
.
More precisely,
we define
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
inductively as follows:

•

c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A):=0
for
A=\Box B
or
A\in{\sf atom}
or
A=\bot
.

•

c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A\circ B):=\max\{c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A),c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B)\}
for
\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge\}
.

•

c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A\to B):=1+\max\{c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A),c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B)\}
.

Then define

c^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A):=\max(\{c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B):\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)\}\cup\{n_{A}\}),

in which
n_{A}
is defined as the number of elements in

{\sf sub}(A)\cap{\sf parb}
. Remember that
{\sf parb}:={\sf par}\cup\{\Box B:B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}\}\cup\{\bot\}
.

One of the best features of the complexity
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
is that
there are only finitely many propositions
A
with
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)\leq n
:

Lemma 2.1
.

Modulo
{\sf IPC}
-provable equivalence relation,
there are finitely many
propositions
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}(X)
with
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)\leq n
, in which

X
is a finite set of atomic or boxed propositions. Moreover
one may effectively compute the finite set of mentioned propositions.

Proof.

By induction on
n
, we define an upper bound
f(n)

for the number of propositions
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}(X)
with

c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)\leq n
. The computability of such set of propositions is left to the reader.

1.

f(0):
Observe that any
A
with
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)=0
is

{\sf IPC}
-equivalent to a disjunction of conjunctions of
propositions in
X
.
Hence
f(0)=2^{2^{m}}
is an obvious upper bound,
in which
m
is the number of propositions in
X
.

2.

f(n+1):
For every implication
B\to C

with
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B\to C)\leq n+1
, we have
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B),c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(C)\leq n
,
and hence
f(n)^{2}
is an upper bound for the number of
inequivalent such propositions. Then since
modulo
{\sf IPC}
-provable equivalence every proposition is a disjunction of conjunctions of propositions in
X
or implications, the following definition is an upperbound:

f(n+1):=2^{2^{[m+f(n)^{2}]}}.\qed

2.6
Kripke models for the intuitionistic modal logics

A Kripke model for the intuitionistic modal logic, is a combination of a Kripke model for intuitionistic logic and the classical modal logic. So as expected, it contains two relations: one (
\preccurlyeq
) for the intuitionistic implication and another (
\sqsubset
) for the modal operator
\Box
or
\rhd
. More precisely, a Kripke model is a quadruple

\mathcal{K}=(W,\prec,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
with the following properties:

•

W\neq\emptyset
.

•

(W,\prec)
is a partial order (transitive and irreflexive). We write
\preccurlyeq
for the reflexive closure of
\prec
.

•

\mathrel{V}
is the valuation on atomics,
i.e.
V\subseteq W\times{\sf atom}
.

•

w\preccurlyeq u
and
w\mathrel{V}a
implies
u\mathrel{V}a
for every
w,u\in W
and
a\in{\sf atom}
.

•

({\preccurlyeq};{\sqsubset})\subseteq{\sqsubset}
,
i.e.
w\preccurlyeq u\sqsubset v
implies

w\sqsubset v
. This condition is assumed to ensure that the previous
property holds for all modal propositions and not only for

a\in{\sf atom}
.

The valuation relation
\mathrel{V}
could be extended to include all modal propositions as follows:

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash a
iff
w\mathrel{V}a
, for
a\in{\sf atom}
.

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A\wedge B
iff
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
and

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
.

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A\vee B
iff
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
or

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
.

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A\to B
iff for every
u\succcurlyeq w
if we have

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
then

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
.

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A\rhd B
iff for every
u\sqsupset w

with
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash A
we have
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
.

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\Box A
iff for every
u\sqsupset w

we have
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
.

We also define the following strengthen of

\Vdash
:

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}A\quad\text{iff}\quad\text{there is some }u\sqsubset w\text{ such that }\mathcal{K},w^{\prime}\Vdash A\text{ for every }w^{\prime}\sqsupset u.

We also define the following notions for Kripke models:

•

Finite:
if
W
is a finite set.

•

Transitive:
if
\sqsubset
is transitive, i.e.
u\sqsubset v\sqsubset w
implies
u\sqsubset w
.

•

Rooted:
if there is some node
w_{0}\in W

such that
w_{0}\mathrel{\widetilde{\sqsubseteq}}w
for every
w\in W
, in which

u\mathrel{\widetilde{\sqsubseteq}}v
iff ther is some
u^{\prime}
such that
u\sqsubseteq u^{\prime}\preccurlyeq v
.

•

Conversely well-founded:
if there is no infinite
ascending sequence
w_{1}\sqsubset w_{2}\sqsubset\ldots
. Note that this condition implies irreflexivity of
\sqsubset
.

•

Tree:
if for every
w\in W
the set

\{u\in W:u\preccurlyeq w\}
is finite linearly ordered
(by
\preccurlyeq
) set.

•

Transcendental:
if
u\sqsubset v
and
w\preccurlyeq v
then

w=v
.

•

{\sf C_{p}}:
if
\mathcal{K}\Vdash{\sf C_{p}}
.

•

Good:
if all of the above properties hold.

•

{\sf C_{a}}:
if
\mathcal{K}\Vdash{\sf C_{a}}
.

Given two Kripke models
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
and
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}=(W^{\prime},\preccurlyeq^{\prime},\sqsubset^{\prime},\mathrel{V}^{\prime})
, we say that

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
is an intuitionistic submodel of
\mathcal{K}

(notation
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{K}
) iff
W=W^{\prime}
,

{\sqsubset}={\sqsubset^{\prime}}
,
V=V^{\prime}
and
{\preccurlyeq^{\prime}}\subseteq{\preccurlyeq}
.
A class
\mathscr{K}
of Kripke models has intuitionistic submodel property,
if
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}

implies
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{K}
.
A modal logic
{\sf T}
is said to have intuitionistic submodel property
iff it is sound and complete for some class
\mathscr{K}
of
good Kripke models with intuitionistic submodel property.

Theorem 2.2
.

{\sf iGL}
is sound and complete for good Kripke models.
Also
{\sf iGLC_{a}}

is sound and complete for good
{\sf C_{a}}
Kripke models.

Proof.

The soundness parts are straightforward and left to the reader.
Also second statement can be easily derived from the first one and left to reader.

Let
{\sf iGL}\nvdash A
for some
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
. We must find some
good Kripke model
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
such that
\mathcal{K}\nVdash A
.
By a standard canonical model construction (see
[
Iemhoff, 2001a
;
Iemhoff, 2001c
]
) one may find a finite Kripeke model

\mathcal{K}_{0}:=(W_{0},\preccurlyeq_{0},\sqsubset_{0},\mathrel{V}_{0})
such that
\mathcal{K}_{0},w_{0}\nVdash A
.
Then define

•

the set of sequences

.

.

It is not difficult to observe that
\mathcal{K}
is a Kripke model indeed
and moreover it is a good Kripke model. Also by a straightforward induction on the complexity of
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we may prove that

\mathcal{K},\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern Lemma 2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
be a finite irreflexive
Kripke model.
Then for every
w_{0}\in W

there is some finite rooted
(
with the root
\langle w_{0}\rangle
)
transcendental tree Kripke model

\mathcal{T}=(W^{\prime},\preccurlyeq^{\prime},\sqsubset^{\prime},\mathrel{V}^{\prime})

which is equivalent to
\mathcal{K}
, i.e. there is a
function
e:W^{\prime}\longrightarrow W
such that

e(\langle w_{0}\rangle)=w_{0}
and for every

w\in W^{\prime}
and
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we have
\mathcal{T},w\Vdash A

iff
\mathcal{K},e(w)\Vdash A
.

Proof.

Define
\mathcal{T}:=(W^{\prime},\preccurlyeq^{\prime},\sqsubset^{\prime},\mathrel{V}^{\prime})
as follows.

•

W^{\prime}:=
the set of finite sequences
(excluding empty sequence)
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}:=\langle w_{0},s_{1},w_{1},s_{2},w_{2},s_{3}\ldots,s_{n},w_{n}\rangle

with the following properties:

–

w_{i}\in W
and
s_{i}\in\{\prec,\sqsubset\}

for every
1\leq i\leq n
.

–

w_{i}\mathrel{s_{i+1}}w_{i+1}
for every

0\leq i<n
. This means that
if
s_{i+1}={\prec}
then
w_{i}\prec w_{i+1}

and if
s_{i+1}={\sqsubset}
then
w_{i}\sqsubset w_{i+1}
.

•

Also define the function
e:W^{\prime}\longrightarrow W
as
follows.
For
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}:=\langle w_{0},s_{1},w_{1},s_{2},w_{2},s_{3}\ldots,s_{n},w_{n}\rangle
define

e(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle w\hfil$\crcr}}}):=w_{n}
. Also
define
e(\langle w_{0}\rangle):=w_{0}
.

•

Let

\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.7
The Gödel’s translation
{(.)}^{\Box}

The following translation, is some variant of the
Gödel’s celebrated translation for
the embedding of
{\sf IPC}
in
{\sf\ S4}

[
Gödel, 1933
]
.

Definition 2.4
.

For every proposition
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
define
A^{\Box}

inductively as follows:

•

{A}^{\Box}:=\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}A
, for
A\in{\sf var}
.

•

{A}^{\Box}:=A
for
A\in{\sf parb}
.

•

{(B\circ C)}^{\Box}:={B}^{\Box}\circ{C}^{\Box}
.
for
\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge\}
.

•

{(B\rightarrow C)}^{\Box}:=\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}({B}^{\Box}\rightarrow{C}^{\Box})
.

A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
is called self complete if there is some

B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
such that
A=B^{\Box}
:

{\sf S}:=\{B^{\Box}:B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}\}.

Also
A
is called
{\sf T}
-complete if
{\sf T}\vdash A\to\Box A
:

{\sf C}^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}:=\{A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}:{\sf T}\vdash A\to\Box A\}.

Note that for every
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
we have

{\sf S}\subseteq{\sf C}^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}
. Whenever no confusion is likely, we may
omit the superscript
{\sf T}
in the notation
{\sf C}^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}
and
simply write
{\sf C}
.

Theorem 2.5
.

{\sf iGL}
and
{\sf iGLC_{a}}
are closed under
(.)^{\Box}
,
i.e. for every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
,

{\sf iGL}\vdash A

(
{\sf iGLC_{a}}\vdash A
)
implies
{\sf iGL}\vdash A^{\Box}

(
{\sf iGLC_{a}}\vdash A^{\Box}
)
.

Proof.

Straightforward induction on the proof

{\sf iGL}\vdash A
(
{\sf iGLC_{a}}\vdash A
)
and left to the reader.
∎

2.8
Notations on set of propositions

In the rest of this
paper we deal with several sets of modal propositions.
For the simplicity of notations, we write

{\sf X_{1}\ldots X_{n}}
for
{\sf X_{1}}\cap\ldots\cap{\sf X_{n}}
,
when
{\sf X_{i}}
are sets of propositions.
For example we write
{\sf S}{\sf N}
for the set of propositions
which are
{\sf N}
(see
section

2.9
) and self-complete (as will be defined in this section).
Also
\subseteq_{\text{fin}}
indicates the finite subset relation.
Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
let
{\sf sub}(A)
be the set of all subformulas of
A
and

{\sf sub_{o}}(A)
is the set of all outer subformulas of
A
:

•

{\sf sub_{o}}(a):=\{a\}
for
a\in{\sf atom}
.

•

{\sf sub_{o}}(\Box A):=\{\Box A\}
.

•

{\sf sub_{o}}(B\circ C):=\{B\circ C\}\cup{\sf sub_{o}}(B)\cup{\sf sub_{o}}(C)
, for
\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge,\to\}
.

Also for arbitrary set
\Gamma
of propositions define

•

{\sf sub}^{\Gamma}(A):={\sf sub}(A)\cap\Gamma
. Hence
{\sf sub^{{\sf B}}}(A):={\sf sub}(A)\cap\{\Box B:B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}\}
.

•

{\sf sub}_{\sf o}^{\Gamma}(A):={\sf sub_{o}}(A)\cap\Gamma
.
Hence
{\sf sub^{{\sf B}}_{o}}(A):={\sf sub_{o}}(A)\cap\{\Box B:B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}\}
.

•

\Gamma^{\vee}:=\{\bigvee\Delta:\Delta\subseteq_{\text{fin}}\Gamma\text{ and }\Delta\neq\emptyset\}
.

•

{\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{{\sf T}}}}{\Gamma}:=
the set
of all
\Gamma
-projective
propositions in the logic
{\sf T}
.
We say that a proposition
A
is
\Gamma
-projective in

{\sf T}
, if there is some substitution
\theta
and

B\in\Gamma
such that
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}B
,
i.e.
{\sf T}\vdash\hat{\theta}(A)\leftrightarrow B
and

{\sf T}\vdash A\to(x\leftrightarrow\theta(x))
for every
x\in{\sf var}
.
Whenever
{\sf T}
may be inferred from context,
we may omit the superscript
{\sf T}
and simply write

{\downarrow}{\Gamma}
.

•

\Gamma(X)
indicates the set

\Gamma\cap\mathcal{L}_{0}(X)
. Also
let
\Gamma(\Box):=\Gamma({\sf parb})
.

Also define

•

{\sf S}:=\{B^{\Box}:B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}\}.

•

{\sf C}^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}:=\{A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}:{\sf T}\vdash A\to\Box A\}.

If
{\sf T}
may be inferred from context, we may omit
the
{\sf T}
-superscript from notation.

•

{\sf P}^{\sf T}:={\sf Prime}^{\sf T}:=
the set of all

{\sf T}
-prime propositions, i.e. the set of propositions
A

such that for every
B,C
with
{\sf T}\vdash A\to(B\vee C)

either we have
{\sf T}\vdash A\to B
or
{\sf T}\vdash A\to C
.
If the logic
{\sf T}
can be inferred from context, we may omit
the superscript
{\sf T}
from notations.

•

Given a set
\Gamma
of propositions,
define
\Gamma\text{-{\sf NF}}_{0}
as the
set of propositions
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
with either

B\in\Gamma
or

\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\in\Gamma
. Then
define
the set
\Gamma\text{-{\sf NF}}
of propositions in
\Gamma
-Normal Form
as follows:

\Gamma\text{-{\sf NF}}:=\{A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}:\forall\,\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)\ B\in\Gamma\text{-{\sf NF}}_{0}\}.

And finally we assume that

(.)^{\vee}
has the lowest precedence and
{\downarrow}{(.)}

has the second lowest precedence.
This means that

{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}:=({\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{\,}}}({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}))^{\vee}\quad\text{and}\quad{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}:=({\sf C}({\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{\,}}}({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)})))^{\vee}.

Lemma 2.6
.

If
\Gamma
is a set of
{\sf T}
-prime propositions
then
{\downarrow}\Gamma

is so.

Proof.

Let
E\in{\downarrow}\Gamma
such that
{\sf T}\vdash E\to(B\vee C)
. Also assume that
E\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}E^{\dagger}\in\Gamma
.
Hence
{\sf T}\vdash E^{\dagger}\to(\hat{\theta}(B)\vee\hat{\theta}(C))
and since

E^{\dagger}
is
{\sf T}
-prime, either we have

{\sf T}\vdash E^{\dagger}\to\hat{\theta}(B)
or
{\sf T}\vdash E^{\dagger}\to\hat{\theta}(C)
.
Hence either we have
{\sf T},E\vdash\hat{\theta}(E\to B)
or
{\sf T},E\vdash\hat{\theta}(E\to C)
. Since
\theta
is
E
-projective, we have
either
{\sf T},E\vdash E\to B
or
{\sf T},E\vdash E\to C
.
∎

2.9
{\sf NNIL}
propositions

The class of
No Nested Implications to the Left
,

{\sf NNIL}
formulae, for the nonmodal language

\mathcal{L}_{0}
, was introduced in

[
Visser et al., 1995
]
, and more explored in
[
Visser, 2002
]
.
Here we gathered all we need from
[
Visser et al., 1995
;
Visser, 2002
]
.
For simplicity of notations, we may write
{\sf N}
for
{\sf NNIL}
.
The crucial
result of
[
Visser, 2002
]
is to provide
an algorithm that as
input, takes
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
and returns its best

{\sf NNIL}
approximation
A^{*}
from below, i.e.,

\vdash A^{*}\rightarrow A
and
for all
{\sf NNIL}
formulae
B
such that
\vdash B\rightarrow A
,
we have
\vdash B\rightarrow A^{*}
. Also for all

\Sigma_{1}
-substitutions
\sigma
,
we have
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash\sigma_{{}_{\sf HA}}(\Box A\leftrightarrow\Box A^{*})

[
Visser, 2002
]
.
The classes
{\sf NNIL}
and
{\sf NI}
of propositions
in
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
are defined inductively:

•

A\in{\sf NNIL}
and
A\in{\sf NI}
for every
A\in{\sf atomb}
.

•

B\circ C\in{\sf NNIL}
if
B,C\in{\sf NNIL}
.
Also
B\circ C\in{\sf NI}
if
B,C\in{\sf NI}
. (
\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge\}
)

•

B\to C\in{\sf NNIL}
if
B\in{\sf NI}
and
C\in{\sf NNIL}
.

Theorem 2.7
.

Let
A\in{\sf NNIL}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{K}
are two Kripke models.
Then
\mathcal{K}\Vdash A
implies
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\Vdash A
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
and
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}=(W,\preccurlyeq^{\prime},\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
.
First by induction on
A\in{\sf NI}
show that
for every
w\in W
we have
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
iff
\mathcal{K}^{\prime},w\Vdash A
.
Then use induction on
A\in{\sf NNIL}
and show that
for every
w\in W
, if
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
then
\mathcal{K}^{\prime},w\Vdash A
.
∎

Lemma 2.8
.

Modulo
{\sf IPC}
-provable equivalence,

{\sf NNIL}(X)
is finite,
whenever
X
is a finite set of atomics or boxed propositions.
Moreover one may effectively compute this finite set.

Proof.

The decidability of
{\sf NNIL}(X)
is deduced by decidability
of
{\sf IPC}
and the following argument for finiteness of
{\sf NNIL}(X)

and left to the reader.
To show that
{\sf NNIL}(X)
is finite, we will find an upper bound

f(n)
for the number of
{\sf IPC}
-inequivalent propositions
in
{\sf NNIL}(X)
with
n
as the number of elements in
X
.

First observe that each proposition in
{\sf NNIL}(X)
can be written
as
\bigvee\bigwedge C
in which
C
is an atomic,
boxed or implication,
which we call it a component.
Hence
f(n)
is less than or equal to
2^{2^{g(n)}}
,
in which
g(n)
is the
number of components in
{\sf NNIL}(X)
.
Then observe that

g(n+1)\leq(n+1)f(n)+n+1
, because one may assume that
each component
C
is either of the form
E\to A
for some

E\in X
and some
A
in
{\sf NNIL}(X\setminus\{E\})
, or
C\in X
. Hence the following (primitive) recursive function is an upperbound for the
number of all formulas in
n
atomics:

f(0):=2\quad\quad,\quad\quad f(n+1):=2^{2^{(n+1)(f(n)+1)}}.\qed

2.10
Admissibility and preservativity

Given a logic
{\sf T}

we say that an inference rule

A

B

is admissible
to
{\sf T}
if
{\sf T}\vdash\theta(A)
implies
{\sf T}\vdash\theta(B)

for every substitution
\theta
. Characterization
of all admissible rules for classical logic is trivial:

A

B

is admissible iff

A\to B
is classically valid. However the case for
intuitionistic logic
{\sf IPC}
or modal logics like

{\sf K4}
are not trivial (see

Iemhoff, 2001b
;
Iemhoff and Metcalfe, 2009
;
Jeřábek, 2005
;
Rybakov, 1987a
;
Rybakov, 1987b
;
Rybakov, 1997
;
Goudsmit and Iemhoff, 2014
;
Iemhoff, 2016
).
In this paper we deal with a generalization
of admissibility. In some sense it is same as admissibility
relative in some set
\Gamma
. This generalization, has been considered in
[
Mojtahedi, 2022a
]
for the propositional language and here in this paper we extend that definition to the modal language.
Given a logic
{\sf T}
and a set
\Gamma
of propositions define

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
iff for every substitution
\theta

and
C\in\Gamma
:

{\sf T}\vdash\hat{\theta}(C\to A)

implies
{\sf T}\vdash\hat{\theta}(C\to B)
.

Also we define
binary relation between propositions
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
,
the preservativity relation,
as follows:

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B\quad\text{ iff }\quad\forall\,E\in\Gamma({\sf T}\vdash E\to A\Rightarrow{\sf T}\vdash E\to B).

[
Iemhoff, 2003
]
studies the preservativity
in the first-order language of arithmetic and axiomatizes it
via the binary modal operator
\rhd
.
Also
[
Visser, 2002
]
studies preservativity for propositional
non-modal language and among other interesting results, it
axiomatizes
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf NNIL}}$}\end{picture}}
.

Remark 2.9
.

By definition it can be inferred that
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
implies

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
, however the converse may not hold. As a counterexample
let
A
and
B
two different variables and
\Gamma:=\{\top\}

and
{\sf T}={\sf IPC}
. Then we have
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
and not
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
.

Lemma 2.10
.

Let
{\sf T}
be a logic which is closed under outer substitutions.
Then
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}^{\!{\sf T}}\!\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}B
.

Proof.

Let
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
and
E\in{\downarrow}\Gamma
with

{\sf T}\vdash E\to A
. Assume that
E\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}E^{\dagger}\in\Gamma
. Since
{\sf T}
is closed under outer substitutions,
we get
{\sf T}\vdash E^{\dagger}\to\hat{\theta}(A)
. Then
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
implies

{\sf T}\vdash E^{\dagger}\to\hat{\theta}(B)
. Hence

{\sf T}\vdash E\to\hat{\theta}(E\to B)
and because

E\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}E^{\dagger}
we get
{\sf T}\vdash E\to B
.
∎

Later in this paper we axiomatize
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
and
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}

for several pairs
({\sf T},\Gamma)
. Before we continue with this, let us see some basic axioms and rules.

Given a logic
{\sf T}
,
the logic
[{\sf T}]

proves statements
A\rhd B
for
A
and
B
in the language of
{\sf T}

and has the following axioms and rules:

Aximos

{\sf Ax}:

A\rhd B
,
for every
{\sf T}\vdash A\to B
.

Rules

A\rhd B

A\rhd C

Conj

A\rhd B\wedge C

A\rhd B

B\rhd C

Cut

A\rhd C

The above mentioned axiom and rules are not interesting, because

[{\sf T}]\vdash A\rhd B
iff
{\sf T}\vdash A\to B
. However we define several interesting additional rules and axioms as follows.
Let
\Delta\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
and define

{\sf Le}
:

A\rhd\Box A
for every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
.

{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}
:

A\rhd\Box A
for every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
.

{\sf A}
:

A\rhd\hat{\theta}(A)
, for every substitution
\theta
.

{\sf V}(\Delta):

B\to C\rhd\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
, in which

B=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\to F_{i})
and

C=\bigvee_{i=n+1}^{n+m}E_{i}
, and

\{A\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(B):=\begin{cases}B\quad&:B\in\Delta\\
A\to B&:\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

B\rhd A

C\rhd A

Disj

B\vee C\rhd A

A\rhd B

(
C\in\Delta
)

\textup{Mont}(\Delta)

C\to A\rhd C\to B

Finally define

[\![{{\sf T},\Delta}]\!]:=[{\sf T}]+\text{Disj}+\textup{Mont}(\Delta)+{\sf V}(\Delta),

[\![{{\sf T},\Delta}]\!]{\sf Le}:=[\![{{\sf T},\Delta}]\!]+{\sf Le}\quad\text{and}\quad[\![{{\sf T},\Delta}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}:=[\![{{\sf T},\Delta}]\!]+{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}.

Remark 2.11
.

{\sf T}\subseteq{\sf T}^{\prime}
and
\Delta\subseteq\Delta^{\prime}
implies

[\![{{\sf T},\Delta}]\!]\subseteq[\![{{\sf T}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}}]\!]
.

Proof.

By induction on the complexity of proof

[\![{{\sf T},\Delta}]\!]\vdash A\rhd A^{\prime}
one must show

[\![{{\sf T}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd A^{\prime}
.
We only treat here the case
{\sf V}(\Delta)
and leave the rest to reader.
So assume that
A=B\to C
and
A^{\prime}=\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
, in which

B=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\to F_{i})
and

C=\bigvee_{i=n+1}^{n+m}E_{i}
. Since for every

D,F\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we have

\vdash\{D\}_{{}_{\!\Delta^{\prime}}}\!(F)\to\{D\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(F)
,
we get
[\![{{\sf T}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}}]\!]\vdash\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta^{\prime}}}\!(E_{i})\rhd\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
. On the other hand by

{\sf V}(\Delta^{\prime})
we have

[\![{{\sf T}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta^{\prime}}}\!(E_{i})
. Thus Cut implies desired result.
∎

Theorem 2.12

(
Soundness
)
.

[{\sf T}]
is sound for preservativity
interpretations,
i.e.
[{\sf T}]\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B

for every set

\Gamma
of propositions and every logic
{\sf T}
. Moreover

1.

if
\Gamma
is
{\sf T}
-complete, then
{\sf Le}
is sound,

2.

if
\Gamma
is
{\sf T}
-prime, then Disj is also sound,

3.

if
\Gamma
is closed under
\Delta
-conjunctions, i.e.
A\in\Gamma
and
B\in\Delta
implies
A\wedge B\in\Gamma

(
up to
{\sf T}
-provable equivalence relation
)
, then
\textup{Mont}(\Delta)

is sound.

4.

if
{\sf T}
has intuitionistic submodel property and

\Gamma\subseteq{\sf NNIL}
and
\Delta\subseteq{\sf atomb}

then
{\sf V}(\Delta)
is sound.

5.

if
\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})

and
{\sf T}
is closed under outer substitutions,
then
{\sf A}
is also sound.

Proof.

Easy induction on the complexity of proof
[{\sf T}]\vdash A\rhd B

and left to the reader. The validity of item 4 is provided
by
section

2.10
.
∎

Theorem 2.13

(
Soundness
)
.

[{\sf T}]
is sound for admissibility
interpretations,
i.e.
[{\sf T}]\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B

for every set

\Gamma
of propositions and every logic
{\sf T}
which is closed under outer substitutions.
Moreover

1.

if
\Gamma
is
{\sf T}
-complete, then
{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}
is sound,

2.

if
\Gamma
is
{\sf T}
-prime, then Disj is also sound.

3.

if
\Gamma
is closed under outer substitutions of

\Delta
-conjunctions, i.e.
A\in\Gamma
and
B\in\Delta
implies
A\wedge\hat{\theta}(B)\in\Gamma

(
up to
{\sf T}
-provable equivalence relation
)
, then
\textup{Mont}(\Delta)

is sound.

4.

if
{\sf T}
has intuitionistic submodel property and

\Gamma\subseteq{\sf NNIL}
and
\Delta\subseteq{\sf parb}

then
{\sf V}(\Delta)
is sound.

Proof.

Easy induction on the complexity of proof
[{\sf T}]\vdash A\rhd B

and left to the reader. The validity of item 4 is provided
by
section

2.10
.
∎

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
{\sf T}
has intuitionistic submodel property
and
\Gamma\subseteq{\sf NNIL}
and
\Delta\subseteq{\sf atomb}
.
Then

B\to C\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
, in which

B=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\to F_{i})
and

C=\bigvee_{i=n+1}^{n+m}E_{i}
. Moreover if
\Delta\subseteq{\sf parb}
then

B\to C\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
.

Proof.

We give a contrapositive reasoning only for

B\to C\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
and leave the similar argument for

B\to C\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
to the reader.
Let us fix some class
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
of rooted Kripke models
with intuitionistic submodel property
for which
{\sf T}
is sound and complete.
Let
E\in\Gamma

be such that
{\sf T}\nvdash E\to(\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i}))
. Hence there is some

\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})\in\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
with the root
w_{0}

such that
\mathcal{K},w_{0}\Vdash E
and
\mathcal{K},w_{0}\nVdash\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
.
Let
I
be the set of indices

i
such that
E_{i}\in\Delta
.
Also let
J
be the complement of
I
. Thus
for every
i\in I
we have
\mathcal{K},w_{0}\nVdash E_{i}

and for every
j\in J
, there is some
w_{j}\succcurlyeq w_{0}

such that
\mathcal{K},w_{j}\Vdash B
and

\mathcal{K},w_{j}\nVdash E_{j}
.
Let
\preccurlyeq^{\prime}
defined on
W
as follows:

{\preccurlyeq^{\prime}}:={\preccurlyeq}\setminus(\{w_{0}\}\times\{v\in W:\neg\exists j\in J(w_{j}\preccurlyeq v)\})

and define
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}:=(W,\preccurlyeq^{\prime},\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
.
Then since
E\in{\sf NNIL}
and
\mathcal{K},w_{0}\Vdash E
,

theorem

2.7

implies
\mathcal{K}^{\prime},w_{0}\Vdash E
.
Moreover, it is not difficult to observe that

\mathcal{K}^{\prime},w_{0}\nVdash E_{i}
for every
i\in I\cup J
.
Hence
\mathcal{K}^{\prime},w_{0}\Vdash B
and
\mathcal{K}^{\prime},w_{0}\nVdash C
.
Thus
\mathcal{K}^{\prime},w_{0}\nVdash E\to(B\to C))
.
Since
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
is assumed to have intuitionistic submodel property, we may conclude
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
and hence

{\sf T}\nvdash E\to(B\to C)
.
∎

Theorem 2.15
.

{\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma^{\vee}}$}\end{picture}}}
and
{\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}={\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma^{\vee}}$}\end{picture}}}\ }
.

Proof.

We only show
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
iff
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma^{\vee}}$}\end{picture}}B
and leave similar
arguments for other statements to the reader.
Right-to-left direction holds since

\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\vee}
.
For other direction assume that

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
and let
E\in\Gamma^{\vee}
such that

{\sf T}\vdash E\to A
. Then
E=\bigvee_{i}E_{i}
with
E_{i}\in\Gamma
.
Hence for every
i
we have
{\sf T}\vdash E_{i}\to A
.
Then
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
implies
{\sf T}\vdash E_{i}\to B
. Thus

{\sf T}\vdash E\to B
, as desired.
∎

2.11
Greatest lower bounds

Given a set
\Gamma\cup\{A\}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
and a logic

{\sf T}
, we say that
B
is a
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-lower bound for

A
if:

1.

B\in\Gamma
,

2.

{\sf T}\vdash B\to A
.

Moreover we say that
B
is the
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-greatest
lower bound (
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-glb) for
A
,
if for every
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-lower bound
B^{\prime}
for
A

we have
{\sf T}\vdash B^{\prime}\to B
. Note that up to
{\sf T}
-provable equivalence relation, such glb is unique
and we annotate it as

\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.

We say that
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
is downward compact, if
every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
has a
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-glb
\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.
If
\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
can be effectively computed,
we say that
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
is recursively downward compact.
A main result in
[
Visser, 2002
]
states that
({\sf NNIL},{\sf IPC})

is recursively downward compact (see section 7 in
[
Visser, 2002
]
).
We say that

(\Gamma,{\sf T})
is (recursively) strong downward compact,
if it is (recursively) downward compact and for every

\Box B\in{\sf sub}(\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}})
either we have

\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)
or
B\in\Gamma\text{-{\sf NF}}_{0}
.
We also inductively define
\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}
:

•

\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{a}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}=a
for every atomic
a
.

•

\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{\ }\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}
commutes with
\{\vee,\wedge,\to\}
.

•

\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{\Box A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}:=\Box\lfloor{\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.

Question 1
.

One may similarly define the notion of least upper bounds
and upward compactness. Does downward compactness
imply upward compactness?

Theorem 2.16
.

B
is the
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-glb for
A
iff

•

B\in\Gamma
,

•

{\sf T}\vdash B\to A
,

•

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
.

Hence we have

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.

Proof.

Left to the reader.
∎

Question 2
.

As we saw in
theorem

2.16
, the glb may be expressed via
preservativity relation
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
. One may think of its
variant
which best suites for lub’s:

A\mathrel{{}^{{}^{*}}\!\!\!\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}B\quad\text{ iff }\quad\forall\,E\in\Gamma({\sf T}\vdash A\to E\Rightarrow{\sf T}\vdash B\to E).

We ask for an axiomatization for
\mathrel{{}^{{}^{*}}\!\!\!\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}
when we let
{\sf T}={\sf IPC}
and

\Gamma={\sf NNIL}
.

Corollary 2.17
.

If
\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
exists, then for every
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we have

{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\to B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B.

Proof.

First assume that
{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
. Also let
E\in\Gamma

such that
{\sf T}\vdash E\to A
.
Theorem

2.16
implies

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
and hence
{\sf T}\vdash E\to\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.
Then by
{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\to B
we get

{\sf T}\vdash E\to B
, as desired.

For the other direction let

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
. By definition we have
\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\in\Gamma

and
{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\to A
. Hence by
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B

we get
{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\to B
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 2.18
.

If
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
is strong downward compact
and
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
,
then for every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we have

\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}\in\Gamma\text{-{\sf NF}}
and

{\sf T}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})\vdash A\leftrightarrow\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}
.

Proof.

Easy induction on the complexity of
A
and left to the reader.
∎

2.12
Modal logics with binary modal operator

A modal logic with a binary modal operator
has been studied in the literature of provability logic at least
for two intended meanings for
A\rhd B
: (1)
{\sf T}+B
is interpretable in
{\sf T}+A

[
Visser, 1990
;
Berarducci, 1990
;
de Jongh and Veltman, 1990
;
Goris and Joosten, 2011
]

and (2)
{\sf T}\vdash E\to A
implies
{\sf T}\vdash E\to B
for every

\Sigma_{1}
sentence
E

[
Iemhoff, 2003
;
Iemhoff et al., 2005
]
.
The first is considered for classical theories like
{\sf T}

and the second is considered for the intuitionistic
theories like
HA
.

[
Iemhoff, 2001c
;
Iemhoff, 2003
]
introduces the
logic
{\sf{iPH}}
(as will be defined in this section)
over the language
\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
.
Albert Visser and Dick de Jongh
prove
[
Iemhoff, 2003
]
that

{\sf{iPH}}
is sound for arithmetical interpretations in
HA
and conjecture that
{\sf{iPH}}
is also complete for such interpretations.
We also conjecture that
{\sf{iPH}}_{\sigma}
(as will be defined in this subsection) is the
\Sigma_{1}
-preservativity logic of
HA
for
\Sigma_{1}
-substitutions, i.e.
{\sf{iPH}}_{\sigma}\vdash A
iff for every
\Sigma_{1}
-substitution

\sigma
we have
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash\sigma_{{}_{\sf HA}}(A)
(see
section

2.3
).

Define
\{\!\!\{{{\sf T},\Delta}\}\!\!\}

as a logic in the language
\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
with following axioms and rules:

Axioms

T
:

All theorems of
{\sf T}
.

{\sf V}(\Delta):

B\to C\rhd\bigvee_{i=1}^{n+m}\{B\}_{{}_{\!\Delta}}\!(E_{i})
, in which

B=\bigwedge_{i=1}^{n}(E_{i}\to F_{i})
and

C=\bigvee_{i=n+1}^{n+m}E_{i}
.

\textup{Mont}(\Delta)
:

A\rhd B\to(C\to A)\rhd(C\to B)

for every
C\in\Delta
.

{\sf Le}
:

A\rhd\Box A
for every
A
.

Disj:

(B\rhd A\wedge C\rhd A)\to(B\vee C)\rhd A
.

Conj:

[(A\rhd B)\wedge(A\rhd C)]\to(A\rhd(B\wedge C))
.

Cut:

[(A\rhd B)\wedge(B\rhd C)]\to(A\rhd C)
.

Rules

MP:

A
,
A\to B
/
B
.

PNec:

A\to B
/
A\rhd B
.

Also define
\{\!\!\{{{\sf T},\Delta}\}\!\!\}^{+}
as
\{\!\!\{{{\sf T},\Delta}\}\!\!\}

plus the following axiom:

{\sf 4p}
:

(B\rhd C)\to\Box(B\rhd C)
.

Then define
{\sf{iPH}}:=\{\!\!\{{{\sf iGL},{\sf parb}}\}\!\!\}
and

{\sf{iPH}}^{+}:=\{\!\!\{{{\sf iGL},{\sf parb}}\}\!\!\}^{+}
. Finally define
{\sf{iPH}}_{\sigma}:=\{\!\!\{{{\sf iGLC_{a}},{\sf atomb}}\}\!\!\}
.

Remark 2.19
.

The Löb’s preservativity principle
(\Box A\to A)\rhd A
,
is derivable in
{\sf{iPH}}
.
This axiom was listed
in the original axiomatization of
{\sf{iPH}}
in
[
Iemhoff, 2003
;
Iemhoff et al., 2005
]
.

Proof.

Let us reason inside
{\sf{iPH}}
.
By
{\sf Le}
we have
(\Box A\to A)\rhd\Box(\Box A\to A)
. Also by
Löb’s axiom in
{\sf iGL}
and necessitation we get

\Box(\Box A\to A)\rhd\Box A
. Thus by Cut we have
(\Box A\to A)\rhd\Box A
. Since
(\Box A\to A)\rhd(\Box A\to A)

by conjunction axiom we have

(\Box A\to A)\rhd(\Box A\wedge(\Box A\to A))
. By
necessitation we have
(\Box A\wedge(\Box A\to A))\rhd A
and thus
Cut implies
(\Box A\to A)\rhd A
.
∎

Theorem 2.20
.

{\sf{iPH}}^{+}
is sound for strong arithmetical interpretations in
HA
, i.e. for
every arithmetical substitution
\alpha
and
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}

with
{\sf{iPH}}^{+}\vdash A
we have
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}(A)

(
see
section

2.3
)
.

Proof.

[
Iemhoff, 2001c
;
Iemhoff, 2003
]
proves that
{\sf{iPH}}\vdash A

implies
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(A)
. The same proof works for

{\sf{iPH}}
and
\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}^{+}(A)
as well. Also the validity of
{\sf 4p}

for strong interpretations is obvious.
∎

2.13
Simultaneous fixed-point theorem

It is well-known that the Gödel-Löb’s logic
GL
proves the fixed-point theorem, i.e.
for every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
and atomic
a
such that

a
only occurs inside the scope of boxes, there is a unique
(up to
GL
-provable equivalence relation)
fixed point for
A
with respect to
a
,
i.e. there is a proposition
D

such that

\hbox{\sf GL}{}\vdash A[a:D]\leftrightarrow D

Moreover one may consider
D
such that it only contains atomics appeared in
A
other than
a
.
A well-known extension of this result is the simultaneous fixed-point theorem, which we state its intuitionistic version in the following theorem.

As we will see in this paper, we mainly deal with outer substitutions
\hat{\theta}
, i.e. we do not substitute variables which are inside scope of boxes. The fixed-point theorem helps us (in the proof of
theorem

5.4
which is major step in the arithmetical completeness of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
) to convert
usual substitutions to outer substitutions.

[
Iemhoff et al., 2005
]
proves the fixed-point theorem
for
{\sf iGL}
. In the following theorem we extend it to a simultaneous version, just like in the classical case.

Theorem 2.21
.

Let
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle E\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle E\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle E\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle E\hfil$\crcr}}}:=\{E_{1},\ldots,E_{m}\}
and
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\}

such that every occurrences of
a_{i}
in
E_{j}
is in the scope of some

\Box
. Then there is a substitution
\tau
which is the simultaneous
fixed point of

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle a\hfil

with respect to

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle E\hfil

in
{\sf iGL}
, i.e.

•

{\sf iGL}\vdash\tau(E_{i})\leftrightarrow\tau(a_{i})
for every
1\leq i\leq m
.

•

\tau
is identity on every atomic
a\not\in{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern {\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(\tau(a_{i}))\subseteq({\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}})
.

Proof.

The classical syntactical proof for this result with
n=1
is valid for its intuitionistic counterpart. We refer the reader to

[
Smoryński, 1985
, theorem 1.3.5]
or

[
Boolos, 1995
, section 8]
.
Then we may use induction on
m
and prove the general case as follows.
As induction hypothesis, assume that we already have the statement of this theorem for
m
and prove it for
m+1
.
So assume that
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle E\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle E\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle E\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle E\hfil$\crcr}}}:=\{E_{1},\ldots,E_{m},E_{m+1}\}
and

{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}}:=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{m},a_{m+1}\}
are given.
By induction hypothesis
there is some substitution
\tau^{\prime}
such that

•

{\sf iGL}\vdash\tau^{\prime}(E_{i})\leftrightarrow\tau^{\prime}(a_{i})
for every
1\leq i\leq m
.

•

\tau^{\prime}
is identity on every atomic
a\not\in({\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern .

•

{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(\tau^{\prime}(a_{i}))\subseteq\{a_{m+1}\}\cup({\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern
