0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}})

for
1\leq i\leq m
.

Then there is a fixed-point
D
for
\tau^{\prime}(E_{m+1})
with respect to

a_{m+1}
, i.e.:

{\sf iGL}\vdash D\leftrightarrow\tau^{\prime}(E_{m+1})[a_{m+1}:D].

Finally define
\tau
as follows:

\tau(a):=\begin{cases}(\tau^{\prime}(a))[a_{m+1}:D]\quad&:a=a_{i}\text{ for }1\leq i\leq m\\
D&:a=a_{m+1}\\
a&:\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

Then it is not difficult to observe that
\tau

satisfies all required conditions for the simultaneous fixed-point.
∎

2.14
Two crucial results

There are two results which are crucial for arithmetical completeness of the provability logic of
HA
:
(1) the characterization of
\Sigma_{1}
-provability
logic of
HA
(
theorem

2.22
) and
(2)
theorem

2.23
which
implies the characterization
of
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}\
and
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Delta}$}\end{picture}}\

for
\Gamma:={{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
and

\Delta:={\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{{\sf IPC}}}}\Gamma
(
section

2.14
).

Theorem 2.22
.

The
\Sigma_{1}
-Provability logic of
HA
is
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}
,
i.e.
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\vdash A
iff
for every
\Sigma_{1}
-substitution
\alpha

we have
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(A)
.

Proof.

[
Ardeshir and Mojtahedi, 2018
]
.
∎

Theorem 2.23
.

Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
, there is a finite set

\Pi\subseteq{{\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{\,}}}{\sf N}}({\sf sub^{{\sf par}}}(A))
such that

1.

{\sf IPC}\vdash\bigvee\Pi\to A
.

2.

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd\bigvee\Pi

(
for definition of

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]
see
section

2.10
)
.

3.

\Pi
is computable as a function of
A
. Moreover for every
D\in\Pi
, the substitution
\theta

with
D\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-5.44446pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf IPC}$}}\hskip-5.44446pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}D^{\dagger}\in{\sf N}({\sf sub^{{\sf par}}}(A))
is computable.

4.

c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B)\leq c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)+1+\#{\sf sub^{{\sf par}}}(A)
for every
B\in\Pi
.

5.

{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(B)\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A)
for every
B\in\Pi
.

Proof.

Theorems 3.12, 3.27 and 4.15 from
[
Mojtahedi, 2022a
]
.
∎

Corollary 2.24
.

Let
{\sf T}:={\sf IPC}
and
\Gamma:={{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
and
\Delta:={\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{{\sf IPC}}}}\Gamma
. Then for every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Delta}$}\end{picture}}B

iff
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}B

iff
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
.

Proof.

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}B
:
One must use induction on the complexity of proof
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
. All cases are easy except for Disj and
{\sf V}^{\sf par}_{\!\text{{\sf AR}}}
, for which we refer the reader to lemma 4.5 in
[
Mojtahedi, 2022a
]
. A similar reasoning for modal logics presented in
theorem

3.39
in this paper.

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Delta}$}\end{picture}}B
:

Section

2.10
.

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Delta}$}\end{picture}}B
implies
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
:
Let
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Delta}$}\end{picture}}B
and
\Pi
be a set of propositions
as provided by
theorem

2.23
. Then for every
E\in\Pi

we have
{\sf IPC}\vdash E\to A
and since

\Pi\subseteq\Delta
we get

{\sf IPC}\vdash E\to B
. Thus
{\sf IPC}\vdash\bigvee\Pi\to B
and

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash\bigvee\Pi\rhd B
. Since
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd\bigvee\Pi

Cut implies
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
.
∎

3
Preservativity and relative admissibility

In this section, we study
preservativity
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
and relative admissibility
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}

for several pairs
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
of sets
\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

and intuitionistic modal logics
{\sf T}
.
The main application is to use them for the axiomatization of provability logic of
HA
and prove its decidability.
More precisely we have the following results in this section.
The main result in
section

3.1
is
theorem

3.2
in which we show that
{\sf NNIL}
propositions can equivalently represented as disjunctions of prime and
{\sf NNIL}
propositions.
In
section

3.2
we define relative projectivity in the modal language. In the rest of subsections in this section we will characterize the following relations:

•

Section

3.3
:
{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \,}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\text{{\sf SPN}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \,}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\text{{\sf SPN}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \,}
, whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\sigma
.

•

Section

3.4
:

{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }
, whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.

•

Section

3.5
:

{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ }
, whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.

•

Section

3.6
:

{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad\ \,}
, whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.

•

Section

3.7
:

{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }
, whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.

•

If
{\sf T}
is decidable, then all above mentioned relations
are also decidable.

Important convention:

All over this section we fix some set

{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf IPC}
of modal propositions which is
closed under modeus ponens and some class
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
of
rooted Kripke models for which
{\sf T}
is sound and complete.
Thus in the rest of this section we may omit
the superscript
{\sf T}
from notations, e.g we may
use shorter notations
{\sf C}
,
{\sf P}
and
{\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{\,}}}\Gamma

instead of
{\sf C}^{\sf T}
,
{\sf P}^{\sf T}
and
{\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{{\sf T}}}}\Gamma
, respectively.

Also we define three types of modal logics as follows.

•

TYPE-0
:
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}

and
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
has extension property (see
section

3.1.2
).

•

TYPE-
\alpha
:
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}

and
{\sf T}
is closed under necessitation
and
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
has extension property
(see
section

3.1.2
) and
the intuitionistic submodel property
(see
section

2.6
) and
{\sf T}
is closed under
outer substitutions, i.e.
A\in{\sf T}

implies
\hat{\theta}(A)\in{\sf T}
for every substitution
\theta
.

•

TYPE-
\sigma
:
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4C_{a}}

and
{\sf T}
is closed under necessitation
and
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
has extension property
(see
section

3.1.2
) and
the intuitionistic submodel property
(see
section

2.6
).

In the rest of this section, in the statements of theorems
the notation (
TYPE-0
) means that
we assumed
{\sf T}
satisfies conditions of

TYPE-0
. We have similar notation for
TYPE-
\alpha
and
TYPE-
\sigma
.

Remark 3.1
.

{\sf iK4}
and
{\sf iGL}
are
TYPE-
\alpha
.
Also
{\sf iK4C_{a}}
and
{\sf iGLC_{a}}
are
TYPE-
\sigma
.
The finite model property for
{\sf iK4}
,
{\sf iGL}
,
{\sf iK4C_{a}}

and
{\sf iGLC_{a}}
implies their decidability.

Proof.

Left to the reader.
∎

In the rest of this paper we may use above remark without mentioning.

3.1
Prime factorization for
{\sf NNIL}

In this section we prove that every
{\sf NNIL}

proposition
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
can be decomposed to a disjunction

\bigvee_{i}A_{i}
of
{\sf T}
-prime
{\sf NNIL}
propositions
(
theorem

3.2
). We take a route similar to the one we had
in section 3.5 of
[
Mojtahedi, 2022a
]
for non-modal language.
To show this, we will need two other
equivalent notions:
{\sf T}
-component and
{\sf T}
-extendible.
We first show that every
A
can be decomposed to

{\sf T}
-components (
section

3.1.1
), and then show that every
{\sf T}
-component is

{\sf T}
-extendible (
section

3.1.2
) and finally show that every
{\sf T}
-extendible is
{\sf T}
-prime (
section

3.1.2
).
We will see in
theorem

3.8
that
these three notions are equivalent.

Remember that
{\sf P}
indicates the set of all

{\sf T}
-prime propositions, i.e.

{\sf P}:=\{A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}:{\sf T}\vdash A\to(B\vee C)\text{ implies }{\sf T}\vdash A\to B\text{ or }{\sf T}\vdash A\to C\}.

Theorem 3.2
.

(
TYPE-0
)

Up to
{\sf iK4}
-provable equivalence relation
we have

{{\sf N}(\Box)}={\sf P}{{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\vee}
and

{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}={\sf S}{\sf P}{{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\vee}
.
Also if
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4C_{a}}
,
up to
{\sf iK4C_{a}}
-provable equivalence relation
we have
{\sf N}={\sf P}{\sf N}^{\vee}
and
{\sf S}{\sf N}={\sf S}{\sf P}{\sf N}^{\vee}
.

Proof.

Direct consequence of

sections

3.1.1
,
3.1.2
and
3.1.2
.
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

(
TYPE-0
)

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }
and
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }
and
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }
and
if
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4C_{a}}
then
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\text{{\sf SPN}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \,}\,
.

Proof.

Direct consequence of
theorems

3.2
and
2.15
.
∎

In the rest of this subsection we have technical lemmas
needed for the proof of above theorem.

3.1.1
{\sf T}
-components

Given
A\in{\sf NNIL}
, we say that
A
is a
{\sf T}
-component
if
A=\bigwedge\Gamma\wedge\bigwedge\Delta

with the following properties:

•

Every
B\in\Gamma
is atomic or boxed.

•

Every
B\in\Delta
is an implication

C\to D
for some atomic or boxed
C
such that

{\sf T}\nvdash\bigwedge\Gamma\to C
.

Let us define
{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}:=\{A\in{\sf NNIL}:E\to F\in{\sf sub_{o}}(A)\text{ implies }E\in{\sf atomb}\}.

In other words,
{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
includes all
{\sf NNIL}
propositions such that
every antecedent occurring outside of boxes is either atomic or boxed.
Obviously every
A\in{\sf NNIL}
(
{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}
)
can be converted to some
A^{\prime}\in{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
(
A^{\prime}\in{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
)
via derivability in
{\sf IPC}
(
{\sf iK4}
).

Lemma 3.4
.

Given
A\in{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}

(
A\in{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
)
,
there is a finite set
\Gamma_{A}\subseteq{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}

(
\Gamma_{A}\subseteq{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
)
of

{\sf T}
-components such that

{\sf T}\vdash A\leftrightarrow\bigvee\Gamma_{A}
and
{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(\Gamma_{A})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
.

Proof.

We use induction on

{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
(the set of atomics or boxed
formulas occurring outside of the scope of boxes in
A
) ordered by
\subset
and find some finite set
\Gamma_{A}\subseteq{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}

(
\Gamma_{A}\subseteq{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
) of

{\sf T}
-components with

{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(\Gamma_{A})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
and
{\sf T}\vdash\bigvee\Gamma_{A}\leftrightarrow A
.

As induction hypothesis assume that for every

{\sf T}^{\prime}\supseteq{\sf IPC}
and

B\in{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
(
B\in{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
) with

{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(B)\subsetneqq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
there is a finite set

\Gamma_{B}\subseteq{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
(
\Gamma_{B}\subseteq{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
) of

{\sf T}^{\prime}
-components such that
{\sf T}^{\prime}\vdash B\leftrightarrow\bigvee\Gamma_{B}

and
{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(\Gamma_{B})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(B)
.
For the induction step, assume that
A\in{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
(
A\in{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
)
is given.
Using derivability in
{\sf IPC}
one may easily find finite sets

\Gamma_{i}
and
\Delta_{i}
such that

•

{\sf IPC}\vdash A\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}
,
in which
A_{i}:=\bigwedge\Gamma_{i}\wedge\bigwedge\Delta_{i}
.

•

\Delta_{i}
includes either atomic or boxed propositions.

•

\Gamma_{i}
includes implications
with atomic or boxed antecedents.

•

{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A_{i})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
.

•

A_{i}\in{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
(
A_{i}\in{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{\!+}
).

It is enough to decompose every
A_{i}
to
{\sf T}
-components.
If
{\sf T}\nvdash\bigwedge\Delta_{i}\to E
for every antecedent

E
of an implication in
\Gamma_{i}
, then
A_{i}
already is a

{\sf T}
-component and we are done. Otherwise, there is some

E\to F\in\Gamma_{i}
such that
{\sf T}\vdash\bigwedge\Delta_{i}\to E
.
Then let
A_{i}^{\prime}:=A_{i}[E:\top]
, i.e. the replacement of every outer occurrences of
E
in
A_{i}
with
\top
. Also let
{\sf T}^{\prime}:={\sf T}+E
.
Hence
{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A^{\prime}_{i})\subsetneqq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
and by induction hypothesis
we may decompose
A^{\prime}_{i}
to
{\sf T}^{\prime}
-components:

{\sf T},E\vdash A^{\prime}_{i}\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j}B_{j}

It is not difficult to observe that if
B_{j}
is a
{\sf T}^{\prime}
-component then

B^{\prime}_{j}:=E\wedge B_{j}
is a
{\sf T}
-component. Moreover

{\sf T}\vdash E\wedge A^{\prime}_{i}\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j}B^{\prime}_{j}
and since

{\sf IPC}\vdash(E\wedge A^{\prime}_{i})\leftrightarrow(E\wedge A_{i})
and
{\sf T}\vdash A_{i}\to E
,
we get

{\sf T}\vdash A_{i}\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{j}B^{\prime}_{j}

Hence we have decomposed
A_{i}
to
{\sf T}
-components
B^{\prime}_{j}
with

{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(B^{\prime}_{j})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
.
∎

Corollary 3.5
.

Every
A\in{\sf NNIL}
can be decomposed to
{\sf T}
-components,
i.e. there is a finite set of

{\sf T}
-components
\Gamma_{A}
such that

{\sf T}\vdash A\leftrightarrow\bigvee\Gamma_{A}
. Moreover:

1.

if
A\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}
then
\Gamma_{A}\subseteq{{\sf N}(\Box)}
,

2.

if
A\in{\sf S}{\sf N}
and
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}

then
\Gamma_{A}\subseteq{\sf S}{\sf N}
,

3.

if
A\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
and
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}

then
\Gamma_{A}\subseteq{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
.

Proof.

Easy consequence of
section

3.1.1
and left to reader. The condition
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
is only needed to convert

A\in{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}
to some
A^{\prime}\in{\sf S}{\sf NNIL}^{+}
.
∎

3.1.2
{\sf T}
-Extension property

Let
\mathscr{K}
and
\mathscr{K}^{\prime}
be two sets of rooted Kripke models.
In the following lines we are going to define a Kripke model

\sum(\mathscr{K},\mathscr{K}^{\prime})
.
Roughly speaking it is resulted by adding a fresh root beneath

\mathscr{K}
having
\sqsubset
-access to the roots of
\mathscr{K}^{\prime}
.
More precisely define

\sum(\mathscr{K},\mathscr{K}^{\prime}):=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
as follows.
Assume that the sets of nodes of Kripke models in

\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{K}^{\prime}
are disjoint.
Moreover we assume that every
\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{K}^{\prime}

is of the form
\mathcal{K}:=(W_{\mathcal{K}},\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{K}},\sqsubset_{\mathcal{K}},\mathrel{V}_{\mathcal{K}})
.

•

W:=\{w_{0}\}\cup\bigcup_{\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{K}^{\prime}}W_{\mathcal{K}}
,
in which
w_{0}\not\in W_{\mathcal{K}}
for every
\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{K}^{\prime}
.

•

\preccurlyeq:=(\bigcup_{\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{K}^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{K}})\cup\{(w_{0},w):\exists\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}\ (w^{\mathcal{K}}_{0}\preccurlyeq_{\mathcal{K}}w)\}
,
in which
w^{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
is the root of
\mathcal{K}
.

•

\sqsubset:=(\bigcup_{\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{K}^{\prime}}\sqsubset_{\mathcal{K}})\cup\{(w_{0},w):\exists\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}^{\prime}\ (w^{\mathcal{K}}_{0}\sqsubseteq_{\mathcal{K}}w)\text{ or }\exists\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}\ (w^{\mathcal{K}}_{0}\sqsubset_{\mathcal{K}}w)\}
.

•

\mathrel{V}:=\bigcup_{\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{K}\cup\mathscr{K}^{\prime}}\mathrel{V}_{\mathcal{K}}
.
Note that this means empty valuation for the fresh root
w_{0}
.

Two Kripke models are said to be
variant
of each other if they share the same rooted frame and their valuations are identical except at the root at which they may have different valuations.

A class
\mathscr{M}
of rooted Kripke models is said to has

extension property
if for every
finite set
\mathscr{K}\subseteq\mathscr{M}
there is some finite set of rooted
Kripke models
\mathscr{K}^{\prime}
such that a variant of
\sum(\mathscr{K},\mathscr{K}^{\prime})

belongs to
\mathscr{M}
. A proposition
A
is said to have

\mathscr{M}
-extension property if
\mathscr{M}\cap\textup{Mod}(A)
has extension property, in which
\textup{Mod}(A)
is the class of all Kripke models of
A
.

Lemma 3.6
.

(
TYPE-0
)

Let
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
be a
{\sf T}
-component
such that
{\sf T}\vdash a\to\Box a
for every

a\in{\sf sub_{o}^{{\sf atom}}}(A)
.
Then
A
has
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
-extension property.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{K}\subseteq\mathscr{M}({\sf T})

be a finite set of Kripke models of
a component
A=\bigwedge\Gamma\wedge\bigwedge\Delta
.
Let

\Gamma=\{a_{1},\ldots,a_{n},\Box E_{1},\ldots,\Box E_{m}\}\quad\text{ and }\quad\Delta=\{b_{1}\to G_{1},\ldots,b_{k}\to G_{k},\Box F_{1}\to H_{1},\ldots\Box F_{l}\to H_{l}\}

in which
a_{i},b_{j}\in{\sf atom}
.
For every
i\leq l
such that
\mathscr{K}\nVdash\Box F_{i}
,
since
{\sf T}\nvdash\bigwedge\Gamma\to\Box F_{i}
,
there is some
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathscr{M}({\sf T})

such that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{i}\Vdash\Gamma
and

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{i}\nVdash\Box F_{i}
. Thus there is some
node
w
such that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{i},w\Vdash\Gamma^{\prime}

in which
\Gamma^{\prime}:=\Gamma\cup\{E_{1},\ldots,E_{m}\}
and

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{i},w\nVdash F_{i}
(note that here we are using

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{i}\Vdash a_{i}\to\Box a_{i}
).
Define
\mathcal{K}_{i}:=(\mathcal{K}_{i}^{\prime})_{w}
, i.e. the Kripke model
generated by
w
in
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{i}
. Let
\mathscr{K}^{\prime}:=\{\mathcal{K}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{K}_{l}\}
and finally define
\mathcal{K}

as a variant of
\sum(\mathscr{K},\mathscr{K}^{\prime})

with the following valuation in the root:

\mathcal{K},w_{0}\Vdash a\quad\text{iff}\quad a\in\Gamma.

Then it is not difficult to observe that

\mathcal{K},w_{0}\Vdash A
.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
with
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
-extension property,
is
{\sf T}
-prime.

Proof.

Let
{\sf T}\nvdash A\to B
and
{\sf T}\nvdash A\to C
.
Then there are Kripke models
\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}\in\mathscr{M}({\sf T})

such that
\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}\Vdash A
and

\mathcal{K}_{1}\nVdash B
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}\nVdash C
.
Since
A
has

\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
-extension property, there is some Kripke model

\mathcal{K}\in\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
which is an extension of

\{\mathcal{K}_{1},\mathcal{K}_{2}\}
and
\mathcal{K}\Vdash A
. Since
\mathcal{K}_{1}\nVdash B
,
we get
\mathcal{K}\nVdash B
.
Similarly
\mathcal{K}\nVdash C
.
Hence
\mathcal{K}\nVdash A\to(B\vee C)
and thus

{\sf T}\nvdash A\to(B\vee C)
.
∎

Theorem 3.8
.

(
TYPE-0
)

Let
A\in{\sf NNIL}

with
{\sf T}\vdash a\to\Box a
for every
a\in{\sf sub_{o}^{{\sf atom}}}(A)
. Following items are equivalent:

1.

A
has
\mathscr{M}({\sf T})
-extension property.

2.

A
is
{\sf T}
-prime.

3.

A
is a
{\sf T}
-component
(
up to

{\sf T}
-provable equivalency
)
.

Proof.

•

1\to 2
:
section

3.1.2
.

•

2\to 3
: Given
A
, first decompose
it to
{\sf T}
-component and then use its
{\sf T}
-primality
and deduce that
A
must be
{\sf T}
-equivalent to one of its
{\sf T}
-components.

•

3\to 1
:
section

3.1.2
.

∎

3.2
Relative projectivity for the modal language

In this section we extend the propositional notion of
relative projectivity

[
Mojtahedi, 2022a
]
to the modal language.

Remember that
\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
indicates the set of modal propositions
with no occurrences of atomic variables outside
\Box
.
Let
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
and
\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
.
A substitution
\theta
is
called
A
-projective
(in
{\sf T}
)
if

(3.1)

\text{For all atomic $a$ we have }\quad{\sf T}\vdash A\to(a\leftrightarrow\theta(a)).

A substitution
\theta
,
is a
\Gamma
-fier
for
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
, if

{\sf T}\vdash\hat{\theta}(A)\in\Gamma\quad\text{i.e.~$\hat{\theta}(A)$ is ${\sf T}$-equivalent to some
$A^{\prime}\in\Gamma$.}

In this case we use the notation
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.00002pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\twoheadrightarrow$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 5.00002pt}}\Gamma
. If

\Gamma
is a singleton
\{A^{\prime}\}
we write

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.00002pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\twoheadrightarrow$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 5.00002pt}}A^{\prime}
instead of
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.00002pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\twoheadrightarrow$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 5.00002pt}}\{A^{\prime}\}
.
The substitution
\theta
is a unifier for
A
if it is

\{\top\}
-fier for
A
.
We say that a substitution
\theta
projects
A
to
\Gamma

in
{\sf T}

(notation:
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}\Gamma
)
if
\theta
is
A
-projective in
{\sf T}
and
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.00002pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\twoheadrightarrow$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 5.00002pt}}\Gamma
.
If
\Gamma=\{A^{\prime}\}
we simplify

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}\{A^{\prime}\}
to
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}A^{\prime}
.
We say that
A
is
\Gamma
-projective in
{\sf T}
(notation

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.389pt}}\Gamma
) if there is some
\theta
such that

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}\Gamma
. Also
{\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{{\sf T}}}}\Gamma

indicates the set of all propositions which are

\Gamma
-projective
in
{\sf T}
. Whenever
{\sf T}
can be inferred from the context,
we may omit it and simply write
{\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{\,}}}\Gamma
.
We say that

A
is projective, if it is
\{\top\}
-projective.

Remark 3.9
.

If
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.389pt}}A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
then
there is some
\tau
such which is identity on every atomic
a\not\in{\sf sub_{o}}(A)
and
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.84393pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau$}}\hskip-1.84393pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.389pt}}A^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
and define

\tau
as follows:

\tau(a):=\begin{cases}\theta(a)\quad&:a\in{\sf sub_{o}}(A)\\
a&:\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Then obviously we have
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.84393pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau$}}\hskip-1.84393pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.389pt}}A^{\prime}
.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

Let
\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
and
{\sf T}

be a modal logic closed under outer substitutions and includes
{\sf IPC}
.
Then for every
\Gamma
-projective proposition
A

in
{\sf T}
,
there is a unique
(
modulo
{\sf T}
-provable equivalency
)

A^{\dagger}\in\Gamma

such that
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.389pt}}A^{\dagger}
. Such
A^{\dagger}

is called the
\Gamma
-projection of
A
in
{\sf T}
.
Moreover
{\sf T}\vdash A\to A^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Let
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}A^{\prime}
and
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.84393pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau$}}\hskip-1.84393pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}}A^{\prime\prime}

and
A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma
.
From the
A
-projectivity of
\theta
and
\tau
,
for every atomic
a
we have
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\theta(a)\leftrightarrow\tau(a)
. Hence
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\hat{\theta}(A)\leftrightarrow\hat{\tau}(A)

and then
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A^{\prime}\leftrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
. By applying

\hat{\theta}
to both sides of this derivation,
we have
\hat{\theta}(A)\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\hat{\theta}(A^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\hat{\theta}(A^{\prime\prime})
.
Since
A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
,
\hat{\theta}(A^{\prime})=A^{\prime}

and
\hat{\theta}(A^{\prime\prime})=A^{\prime\prime}
and thus
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A^{\prime}\to A^{\prime\prime}
.
Similarly we have
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A^{\prime\prime}\to A^{\prime}
.

Next we show
{\sf T}\vdash A\to A^{\dagger}
.
Let
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}A^{\dagger}
. Then
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A\leftrightarrow\hat{\theta}(A)
which implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A\leftrightarrow A^{\dagger}

and hence
{\sf T}\vdash A\to A^{\dagger}
.
∎

3.3
{\sf S}{\sf N}
-Preservativity

[
Visser, 2002
]
axiomatizes the binary relation of

{\sf NNIL}
-preservativity for the non-modal language
and
{\sf IPC}
(
theorem

3.11
).
Here we will do a similar job for the modal language
(
theorem

3.13
) and show that
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}

axiomatizes
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\
when
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\sigma
.
Moreover we also show that
({\sf S}{\sf NNIL},{\sf T})
is
recursively downward compact when
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\sigma
.

Theorem 3.11
.

For every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
,
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
iff
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf NNIL}}$}\end{picture}}\ \,B
. Moreover,
for every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

there is some
A^{*}\in{\sf NNIL}

which is effectively computable and

{\sf IPC}\vdash A^{*}\to A
and
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{*}

and moreover
{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A^{*})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
.

Proof.

[
Visser, 2002
]
.
∎

Theorem 3.12
.

(
TYPE-
\sigma
)

Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we have
A^{\sf h}:=(A^{*})^{\Box}=\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\sf S}{\sf N}}}}

and hence
({\sf S}{\sf N},{\sf T})
is recursively strong downward compact

(
see
section

2.11
)
.

Proof.

First we show that
A^{\sf h}:=(A^{*})^{\Box}
is the
({\sf S}{\sf N},{\sf T})
-glb
for
A
, in which
A^{*}
is as provided by

theorem

3.11
.
By
theorem

3.11
,
A^{*}\in{\sf NNIL}

and
{\sf IPC}\vdash A^{*}\to A
. Also
we have
A^{\sf h}=(A^{*})^{\Box}\in{\sf S}{\sf N}
and

{\sf iK4C_{a}}\vdash A^{\sf h}\to A^{*}
.

Section

3.3
implies

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf h}
and then

theorem

3.18
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ A^{\sf h}
.
Thus
theorem

2.16
implies

A^{\sf h}=\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\sf S}{\sf N}}}}
.

Theorem

3.11
implies that

A^{*}
is computable and hence
\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\sf S}{\sf N}}}}=A^{\sf h}
is so.
Next we reason for the strongness.

Theorem

3.11
implies that

{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A^{*})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}_{o}}(A)
, and hence for
every boxed subformula
\Box B
of
A^{\sf h}:=(A^{*})^{\Box}
either
we have
\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)
or
B\in{\sf S}{\sf N}\text{-{\sf NF}}_{0}
.
∎

Theorem 3.13
.

(
TYPE-
\sigma
)

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}
is sound and complete for
{\sf S}{\sf N}
-preservativity in
{\sf T}
. More precisely,
for every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
:

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ B.

Proof.

The left-to-right direction (soundness) holds by
theorem

3.18
.

For the other direction, let
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ B
.

Theorems

3.12
and
2.11

implies
{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf h}\to B
and hence

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A^{\sf h}\rhd B
.
Also by
section

3.3

we have
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf h}
and then
Cut implies desired result.
∎

Corollary 3.14
.

(
TYPE-
\sigma
)

{\sf T}
is closed under
(.)^{\sf h}
, i.e. if

{\sf T}\vdash A
then
{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf h}
.

Proof.

Let
{\sf T}\vdash A
and hence
{\sf T}\vdash\top\to A
.

Sections

3.3
and
2.10
implies
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf h}
.
Then
theorem

3.13
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ A^{\sf h}
and hence

{\sf T}\vdash\top\to A^{\sf h}
.
∎

Lemma 3.15
.

For every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we have
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf h}
.

Proof.

First note that by

theorem

3.11
we have

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{*}
and by

section

2.10

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{*}
. In the other hand

section

3.3
implies

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A^{*}\rhd(A^{*})^{\Box}
. Then Cut
implies desired result.
∎

Lemma 3.16
.

For every
A\in{\sf NNIL}
, we have
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\Box}
.

Proof.

Use induction on the complexity of
A
:

•

A
is atomic. Then
A^{\Box}=A\wedge\Box A
and by

Le
we have
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd\Box A
. Since

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A
, Conj implies the desired result.

•

A
is boxed. Then
A^{\Box}=A
and
hence
{\sf iK4C_{a}}\vdash A\to A^{\Box}
. Thus

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\Box}
.

•

A=B\wedge C
. By induction hypothesis,

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash B\rhd B^{\Box}
and
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash C\rhd C^{\Box}
. On the other hand,

{\sf T}\vdash(B\wedge C)\to B
and

{\sf T}\vdash(B\wedge C)\to C
. Hence

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(B\wedge C)\rhd B
and

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(B\wedge C)\rhd C
, which by Cut and Conj
we have

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(B\wedge C)\rhd(B^{\Box}\wedge C^{\Box})
. Since

(B\wedge C)^{\Box}=B^{\Box}\wedge C^{\Box}
,
we have the desired result.

•

A=B\vee C
. By induction hypothesis,

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash B\rhd B^{\Box}
and
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash C\rhd C^{\Box}
.
On the other hand,

{\sf T}\vdash B\to(B\vee C)
and

{\sf T}\vdash C\to(B\vee C)
. Hence

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash B\rhd(B\vee C)
and

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash C\rhd(B\vee C)
, which by Disj and Cut we have

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(B\vee C)\rhd(B^{\Box}\vee C^{\Box})
. Since

(B\vee C)^{\Box}=B^{\Box}\vee C^{\Box}
,
we have the desired result.

•

A=B\to C
. By induction hypothesis
we have
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash C\rhd C^{\Box}
.
Since
A\in{\sf NNIL}
, we have
B\in{\sf NI}
and

section

3.3
implies
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(B\to C)\rhd(B\to C^{\Box})
.
Since
{\sf iK4C_{a}}\vdash B\leftrightarrow B^{\Box}
, we get

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(B\to C^{\Box})\rhd(B^{\Box}\to C^{\Box})
. Finally
by
Le
we get
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(B^{\Box}\to C^{\Box})\rhd\Box(B^{\Box}\to C^{\Box})

and hence
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(B\to C)\rhd(B\to C)^{\Box}
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 3.17
.

The following rule is admissible to
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}
:

A\rhd B

E\in{\sf NI}

E\to A\rhd E\to B

.

Proof.

Use induction on the complexity of
E
.

•

If
E
is atomic or boxed, then
it is same as the Montagna’s rule.

•

E=E_{1}\wedge E_{2}
. Let
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
.
Then by induction hypothesis we have
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(E_{2}\to A)\rhd(E_{2}\to B)
. Again by induction hypothesis
we have
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(E_{1}\to(E_{2}\to A))\rhd(E_{1}\to(E_{2}\to B))
and thus by Cut and
{\sf iK4C_{a}}
we get

[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash E\to A\rhd E\to B
.

•

E=E_{1}\vee E_{2}
. Let
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
.
Then by induction hypothesis we have
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(E_{1}\to A)\rhd(E_{1}\to B)
and
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash(E_{2}\to A)\rhd(E_{2}\to B)
.
Hence
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash((E_{1}\to A)\wedge(E_{2}\to A))\rhd((E_{1}\to B)\wedge(E_{2}\to B))
. Thus
[\![{{\sf iK4C_{a}},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash E\to A\rhd E\to B
, as desired.
∎

Theorem 3.18

(
Soundness
)
.

(
TYPE-
\sigma
)

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ B
.

Proof.

Let
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
.

Theorem

2.12

implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\text{{\sf SPN}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \,B
and then
section

3.1
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ B
.
∎

In the following corollary, we consider
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}
as a binary relation which it axiomatizes.

Corollary 3.19
.

(
TYPE-
\sigma
)

The following equalities hold. Moreover if
{\sf T}
is decidable,
all above relations are decidable.

{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf atomb}}]\!]{\sf Le}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \,}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\text{{\sf SPN}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \,}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\text{{\sf SPN}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \,}

Proof.

Theorems

3.13
,
3.1
and
2.15
imply equalities.
For the decidability of
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf S}{\sf N}}$}\end{picture}}\
we have following argument.

Theorem

3.12
implies that

\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\sf S}{\sf N}}}}
exists and is computable.
Then by
section

2.11
it is enough to decide

{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\sf S}{\sf N}}}}\to B
, which is provided by decidability of
{\sf T}
.
∎

3.4
{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
-Preservativity and
{{\sf N}(\Box)}
-Admissibility

In this section we show that
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]
axiomatizes
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \

and
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \
whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.
Moreover we show that
({\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee},{\sf T})

is recursively strong downward compact whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.

Lemma 3.20
.

Let
\alpha
be a general
substitution such that for every

p\in{\sf par}
we have
\alpha(p)\in{\sf parb}
.
Then for every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
:

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B\quad\text{implies}\quad[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash\alpha(A)\rhd\alpha(B).

Proof.

Easy induction on the complexity of proof
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
and left to the reader.
∎

0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle q\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern .
Thus for
\theta:=\alpha\circ\theta_{0}
we have

\alpha(D_{0})=D\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-5.44446pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf IPC}$}}\hskip-5.44446pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}D^{\dagger}=\alpha(D_{0}^{\dagger})\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}
.
Then define

A_{0}^{\sf p}:=\bigvee\Pi^{0}_{A}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\Pi^{1}_{A}:=\{\alpha(B):B\in\Pi^{0}_{A}\}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad A^{\sf p}_{1}:=\bigvee\Pi^{1}_{A}.

Note that by P3, one may effectively compute
\Pi^{0}_{A}
,
\Pi^{1}_{A}

and
A^{\sf p}_{1}
.

Lemma 3.22
.

Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we have:

1.

\Pi^{1}_{A}\subseteq{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
and hence
A^{\sf p}_{1}\in{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}
.

2.

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf p}_{1}
.

Proof.

1.

We show that for
every
D\in\Pi^{1}_{A}
we have
D\in{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
.

D=\alpha(D_{0})
and

with
\alpha

as in
section

3.4
.
Hence

N}({\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern Then if we let

, we have

.

2.

P2 in

section

3.4
implies
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]\vdash A_{0}\rhd A_{0}^{\sf p}

and

3.4
implies

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf p}_{1}
.
∎

Lemma 3.23
.

Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
,
for every
\Box E\in{\sf sub_{o}}(A^{\sf p}_{1})

we have
\Box E\in{\sf sub_{o}}(A)
.

Item P5 in

3.4
.
∎

Lemma 3.24
.

Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
, for every
D\in\Pi^{1}_{A}
we have

c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(D)\leq{c}^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
.
(
see
section

2.5
)

Proof.

If
D=\alpha(D_{0})
with
D_{0}\in\Pi^{0}_{A}
, by P4 in

section

3.4
we have
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(D_{0})\leq{c}^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
.
Also we have
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(D_{0})=c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(\alpha(D_{0}))
and thus

c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(D)\leq{c}^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
.
∎

Theorem 3.25
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

({\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee},{\sf T})
is recursively strong downward compact and

A^{\sf p}_{1}=\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}
.

Proof.

Recursively strong downward compactness
is derived from definition of
A^{\sf p}_{1}
once
we show
\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}=A^{\sf p}_{1}
.
Hence by
theorem

2.16
it is enough to show the following items:

•

A^{\sf p}_{1}\in{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}
: First item of
section

3.4
.

•

{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf p}_{1}\to A
: By P1 in

section

3.4
for every
D_{0}\in\Pi^{0}_{A}

we have
{\sf IPC}\vdash D_{0}\to A_{0}
.
Hence
{\sf IPC}\vdash D\to A
for every
D\in\alpha(\Pi^{0}_{A})
.

•

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ A^{\sf p}_{1}
: By the second item of

sections

3.4
and
2.10
we have

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf p}_{1}
. Then
theorem

3.28
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ A^{\sf p}_{1}
.
Thus by
theorem

2.15
we are done. ∎

Corollary 3.26
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

{\sf T}
is closed under
(.)^{\sf p}_{1}
, i.e. if

{\sf T}\vdash A
then
{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf p}_{1}
.

Proof.

Let
{\sf T}\vdash A
and hence
{\sf T}\vdash\top\to A
.

Theorem

3.25
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ A^{\sf p}_{1}
, and
since
\top\in{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
, we have

{\sf T}\vdash\top\to A^{\sf p}_{1}
.
∎

Theorem 3.27
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

For every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
:

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B.

Proof.

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ B
:

theorem

3.28
.

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
:
since
{\sf T}
is closed under outer substitutions,

section

2.10
implies desired result.

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
implies
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
:
let

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
.

Sections

2.11
and
3.25
implies
{\sf T}\vdash A_{1}^{\sf p}\to B
and hence

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A_{1}^{\sf p}\rhd B
.
On the other hand,
section

3.4
implies

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd A_{1}^{\sf p}
. Thus
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
, as desired.
∎

Theorem 3.28

(
Soundness
)
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ B
.

Proof.

Let
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash A\rhd B
.

Theorem

2.13

implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
and then
section

3.1
implies the

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ B
.
∎

Lemma 3.29
.

(
TYPE-0
)

Up to
{\sf T}
-provable equivalence we have

{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}={\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}
.

Proof.

Since
{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}\subseteq{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
, we have
{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}\subseteq{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}
.
For other direction, let
B\in{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
. Hence there is some
substitution
\theta
such that

B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}B^{\dagger}\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}
and by

theorem

3.2
there are propositions
B^{\dagger}_{i}\in{{\sf PN}(\Box)}

such that
{\sf iK4}\vdash B^{\dagger}\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{i}B^{\dagger}_{i}
.
Then let

B_{i}:=B\wedge B^{\dagger}_{i}
. Since
{\sf T}\vdash B\to B^{\dagger}
,
we have
{\sf T}\vdash B\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{i}B_{i}
.
Then one may easily observe that
B_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}B^{\dagger}_{i}\in{{\sf PN}(\Box)}
and hence
B_{i}\in{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}
, as desired.
∎

In the following corollary, we consider
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]
as binary relation which it axiomatizes.

Corollary 3.30
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

The following equalities hold. Moreover if
{\sf T}
is decidable,
all above relations are decidable.

{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }

Proof.

Theorem

3.27
implies
{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }
.
On the other hand
theorem

2.15
implies

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ }
and
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ \ }
and
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }
and
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }
.
Section

3.4
implies

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ \ }
and also
theorem

3.2
implies

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf PN}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }
.

Next we show decidability of
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ B
.

Theorem

3.25
implies that

\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}
exists and is computable.
Then by
section

2.11
it is enough to decide

{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}\to B
, which is provided by decidability of
{\sf T}
.
∎

3.5
{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
-Preservativity and
{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
-Admissibility

In this section we show that
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}
axiomatizes
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\

and
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \
whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.
Moreover we show that
({{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf T})

is recursively strong downward compact whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.

Lemma 3.31
.

For every
A\in{{\sf NNIL}(\Box)}
, we have
[\![{{\sf iK4},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd A^{\Box}
.

Proof.

Use induction on the complexity of
A
. All cases are similar to the proof of
section

3.3
and left to the reader.
∎

Definition 3.32
.

For every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
define
A^{\sf a}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

and
\Pi^{\prime}_{A}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
as follows.

\Pi^{\prime}_{A}:=\{D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}:D\in\Pi^{1}_{A}\}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad A^{\sf a}:=\bigvee\Pi^{\prime}_{A}.

Note that by
section

3.4
,
one may effectively compute
\Pi^{\prime}_{A}

and
A^{\sf a}
.

Lemma 3.33
.

If
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
and

B\in{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
then
B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}\in{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
and

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash B\rhd B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}
.

Proof.

First observe that
B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}B^{\dagger}
implies

B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}
. Hence if

B^{\dagger}\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}
then

(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
.
Then
section

3.2

implies
{\sf T}\vdash B\to B^{\dagger}
and hence

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]\vdash B\rhd B^{\dagger}
.
Thus by
sections

3.5
and
2.10
we have

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash B\rhd(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}
.
∎

Lemma 3.34
.

Let
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
and
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
. Then

1.

\Pi^{\prime}_{A}\subseteq{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
and hence

A^{\sf a}\in{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
.

2.

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf a}
.

Proof.

Sections

3.4
and
3.5
.
∎

Lemma 3.35
.

Let
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
and

B
is a glb for
A
with respect to
({\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee},{\sf T})
.
Also assume that
B=\bigvee\Pi
with
\Pi\subseteq{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
.
Then
\bigvee\check{\Pi}
is a glb for
A
with respect to

({{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf T})
in which
\check{\Pi}:=\{D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}:D\in\Pi\}
.

Proof.

By
theorem

2.16
it is enough to show the following items for arbitrary
D\in\Pi
:

•

D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}\in{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
:

Section

3.5
.

•

{\sf T}\vdash[D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}]\to A
:
By assumption we have

{\sf T}\vdash D\to A
. Then since

\vdash[D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}]\to D

we get
{\sf T}\vdash[D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}]\to A
.

•

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ \bigvee\check{\Pi}
:
By assumption we have

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ \bigvee\Pi
. Also
section

3.5

implies
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash\bigvee\Pi\rhd\bigvee\check{\Pi}
.
Thus
theorem

3.39
implies

\bigvee\Pi\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \bigvee\check{\Pi}

and hence
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \bigvee\check{\Pi}
.
Then by
theorem

2.15
we have

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ \bigvee\check{\Pi}
.∎

Theorem 3.36
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

({{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf T})
is recursively strong downward compact and

A^{\sf a}=\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}
.

Proof.

Theorems

3.25
and
3.5
.
∎

Corollary 3.37
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

{\sf T}
is closed under
(.)^{\sf a}
, i.e. if

{\sf T}\vdash A
then
{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf a}
.

Proof.

Let
{\sf T}\vdash A
and hence
{\sf T}\vdash\top\to A
.

Theorem

3.36
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ A^{\sf a}
, and
since
\top\in{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
, we have

{\sf T}\vdash\top\to A^{\sf a}
.
∎

Theorem 3.38
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

For every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
:

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ B.

Proof.

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
:

theorem

3.39
.

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ B
:
since
{\sf T}
is closed under outer substitutions,

section

2.10
implies desired result.

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ B
implies
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd B
:
let

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ B
.
Sections

2.11
and
3.36
implies
{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf a}\to B

and hence
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A^{\sf a}\rhd B
.
On the other hand,
section

3.5
implies

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf a}
. Thus
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd B
.
∎

Theorem 3.39

(
Soundness
)
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
.

Proof.

Let
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}\vdash A\rhd B
.

Theorem

2.13
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ B
and
section

3.1
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
.
∎

Lemma 3.40
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)

Up to
{\sf T}
-provable equivalence we have

{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}={{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
and
{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}={{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}^{\!\vee}}
.

Proof.

We only reason for the first equality and leave similar argument for
second one to the reader.
Since
{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}\subseteq{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
, we have

{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}\subseteq{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
.
For other direction, let
B\in{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
.
Hence there is some
substitution
\theta
such that

B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}B^{\dagger}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
and by

theorem

3.2
there are propositions
B^{\dagger}_{i}\in{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}

such that
{\sf iK4}\vdash B^{\dagger}\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{i}B^{\dagger}_{i}
.
Then let

B_{i}:=B\wedge B^{\dagger}_{i}
. Since
{\sf T}\vdash B\to B^{\dagger}
,
we have
{\sf T}\vdash B\leftrightarrow\bigvee_{i}B_{i}
.
Then one may easily observe that
B_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}B^{\dagger}_{i}\in{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}
and hence
B_{i}\in{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}
.
∎

In the following corollary, we consider
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}
as binary relation which it axiomatizes.

Corollary 3.41
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
The following equalities hold. Moreover if
{\sf T}
is decidable,
all above relations are decidable.

{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ }

Proof.

Theorem

3.38
implies
{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}^{{}^{-}}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }
. On the other hand

theorem

2.15
implies
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }
and

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}
and
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }
and

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ }
. Moreover
theorem

3.2
implies

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ }
.
Finally
