section

3.5
implies

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}
.

Next we show decidability of
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ B
.

Theorem

3.36
implies that

\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}
exists and is computable.
Then by
section

2.11
it is enough to decide

{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}\to B
, which is provided by decidability of
{\sf T}
.
∎

3.6
{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
-Preservativity

In this section we show that
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}
axiomatizes
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \

whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
. Moreover we show that
({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf T})

is recursively strong downward compact whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.

Definition 3.42
.

For every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we define
A^{\sf p}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

and
\Pi_{A}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
as follows.

\Pi_{A}:=\{\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}:D\in\Pi^{1}_{A}\}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad A^{\sf p}:=\bigvee\Pi_{A}.

Note that by
section

3.4
,
one may effectively compute
\Pi_{A}

and
A^{\sf p}
.

Lemma 3.43
.

If
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
and

B\in{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
then
\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
and

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash B\rhd\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}
.

Proof.

First observe that
B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}B^{\dagger}\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}

implies

\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-2.38194pt\raisebox{-5.99649pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf T}$}}\hskip-2.38194pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}\Box B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}
. Hence if

B^{\dagger}\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}
then

\Box B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}

and thus
\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
.

Section

3.5
implies
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash B\rhd(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}
.
Also by Leivant’s principle we have
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash B\rhd\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B

and thus

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash B\rhd\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\wedge(B^{\dagger})^{\Box}
.
∎

Lemma 3.44
.

Let
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
and
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
. Then we have:

1.

\Pi_{A}\subseteq{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
and hence

A^{\sf p}\in{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
.

2.

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf p}
.

Proof.

Sections

3.4
and
3.6
.
∎

Lemma 3.45
.

Let
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf iK4}
and
B
is a glb for
A
with respect to
({\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee},{\sf T})
.
Also assume that
B=\bigvee\Pi
with
\Pi\subseteq{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
.
Then

\bigvee\hat{\Pi}

is glb for
A
with respect to

({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf T})
, in which
\hat{\Pi}:=\{\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}:D\in\Pi\}
.

Proof.

By
theorem

2.16
it is enough to show the following items for arbitrary
D\in\Pi
:

•

\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
:
Section

3.6
.

•

{\sf T}\vdash[\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}]\to A
:
By assumption we have

{\sf T}\vdash D\to A
. Then since
\vdash[\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}]\to D

we get
{\sf T}\vdash[\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}]\to A
.

•

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad\bigvee\hat{\Pi}
:
By assumption we have

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ \ \bigvee\Pi
. Also
section

3.6

implies
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash\bigvee\Pi\rhd\bigvee\hat{\Pi}
.
Thus
theorem

3.49
implies

\bigvee\Pi\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ \bigvee\hat{\Pi}

and hence
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ \bigvee\hat{\Pi}
.
Then by
theorem

2.15
we have

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad\bigvee\hat{\Pi}
.∎

Theorem 3.46
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf T})
is recursively strong downward compact and

A^{\sf p}=\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}
.

Proof.

Theorems

3.25
and
3.6
.
∎

Corollary 3.47
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
{\sf T}
is closed under
(.)^{\sf p}
, i.e. if

{\sf T}\vdash A
then
{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf p}
.

Proof.

Let
{\sf T}\vdash A
and hence
{\sf T}\vdash\top\to A
.

Theorem

3.46
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ A^{\sf p}
, and
since
\top\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
, we have

{\sf T}\vdash\top\to A^{\sf p}
.
∎

Theorem 3.48
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
For every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
:

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ B.

Proof.

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ B
:

theorem

3.49
.

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ B
implies
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
:
let

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ B
.
Sections

2.11
and
3.46
imply
{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf p}\to B
and hence
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A^{\sf p}\rhd B
.
On the other hand,
section

3.6
implies

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf p}
. Thus
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
.
∎

Theorem 3.49

(
Soundness
)
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ B
.

Proof.

Let
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd B
.
Theorem

2.12
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad B
and thus
section

3.5
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ B
.
∎

In the following corollary, we consider
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}
as binary relation which it axiomatizes.

Corollary 3.50
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad\ \,}
.
Moreover if
{\sf T}
is decidable,
all mentioned relations are decidable.

Proof.

Theorem

3.48
implies
{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }
.
On the other hand
theorem

2.15
implies

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}
and
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad\ \,}
.
Finally
section

3.5
implies

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{{\downarrow}{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad\ \,}
.

Next we show decidability of
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\quad B
.

Theorem

3.46
implies that

\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}
exists and is computable.
Then by
section

2.11
it is enough to decide

{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}\to B
,
which is provided by decidability of
{\sf T}
.
∎

Lemma 3.51
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A
implies
{\sf{iPH}}\vdash A
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on the proof complexity of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A
that
{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}\vdash A
.
All cases are trivial except for when
A
is an axiom instance of

{\sf H}({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}},{{\sf iGL}})
, i.e.
A=\Box B\to\Box C
with

B\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf iGL}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ C
.
Section

3.6
implies that

[\![{{\sf iGL},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash B\rhd C
. Also from definition of
[\![{{\sf iGL},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}

(see
section

2.10
) it is clear that
{\sf{iPH}}\vdash B\rhd C
.
Then Cut implies
{\sf{iPH}}\vdash\top\rhd B\to\top\rhd C
.
Thus
{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}\vdash\Box B\to\Box C
.
∎

3.7
{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
-Preservativity

In this section we show that
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}
axiomatizes
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \

whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
. Moreover we show that
({{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}},{\sf T})

is recursively strong downward compact whenever
{\sf T}
is
TYPE-
\alpha
.

Theorem 3.52
.

For every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
one may effectively compute

A^{\star}\in{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
such that:

1.

{\sf IPC}\vdash A^{\star}\to A
,

2.

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd A^{\star}
,

3.

{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A^{\star})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A)
.

Proof.

See lemma 4.23 in
[
Mojtahedi, 2022a
]
.
∎

Remember that
{\sf parb}
is the set of parameters or boxed propositions.

Lemma 3.53
.

Let
\alpha
be a substitution such that
for every
a\in{\sf atom}
if
\alpha(a)\neq a

then
a\in{\sf par}
and
\alpha(a)\in{\sf parb}
.
Then

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd B
implies

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf A}\vdash\alpha(A)\rhd\alpha(B)
, for every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
.

Proof.

Straightforward induction on the proof
[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd B

and left to reader.
∎

Lemma 3.54
.

For every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
one may effectively compute some

A^{\star\!\star}\in{{\sf NNIL}(\Box)}
such that:

1.

{\sf IPC}\vdash A^{\star\!\star}\to A
,

2.

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd A^{\star\!\star}
,

3.

{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A^{\star\!\star})\subseteq{\sf sub}(A)
.

Proof.

Let
\Box B_{1},\ldots,\Box B_{n}
be the list of all outer
occurrences of boxed formulas in
A
.
Moreover let
{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}}:=p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}
be a list of fresh atomic parameters, i.e.
p_{i}\not\in{\sf sub}(A)
and they are pairwise distinct.
Also assume that

\alpha
be the substitution such that

\alpha(a):=\begin{cases}\Box B_{i}\quad&:\text{ if }a=p_{i},\\
a&:\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Then there is a unique
A_{0}\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
such that
\alpha(A_{0})=A
.

Theorem

3.52
give us some
A_{0}^{\star}\in{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
such that

1.

{\sf IPC}\vdash A_{0}^{\star}\to A_{0}
,

2.

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf par}}]\!]{\sf A}\vdash A_{0}\rhd A_{0}^{\star}
,

3.

{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A_{0}^{\star})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A_{0})
.

Define
A^{\star\!\star}:=\alpha(A_{0}^{\star})
. Then we have:

1.

{\sf IPC}\vdash A^{\star\!\star}\to A
,

2.

By
section

3.7
we have

[\![{{\sf IPC},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd A^{\star\!\star}
,

3.

{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A^{\star\!\star})\subseteq{\sf sub}(A)
.∎

Theorem 3.55
.

For every
A
there is some
A^{\sf s}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
such that:

1.

{\sf iK4}\vdash A^{\sf s}\to A
,

2.

[\![{{\sf iK4},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf s}
,

3.

\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A^{\sf s})
implies either

\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)
or
\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
.

4.

{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A^{\sf s})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf atom}}}(A)
.

Proof.

By
section

3.7
there is some
A^{\star\!\star}
with
mentioned properties. Since
A^{\star\!\star}\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}
we have

{\sf iK4}\vdash(A^{\star\!\star})^{\Box}\to A^{\star\!\star}
. Also

section

3.5
implies

[\![{{\sf iK4},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A^{\star\!\star}\rhd(A^{\star\!\star})^{\Box}
.
Hence if we let
A^{\sf s}:=(A^{\star\!\star})^{\Box}
, by
section

3.7

we have all required properties.
∎

Theorem 3.56
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf T})
is recursively strong downward compact and

A^{\sf s}:=(A^{\star\!\star})^{\Box}=\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{\sf S}{\sf N}}}}
.

Proof.

We show that
A^{\sf s}
is the
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf T})
-glb for
A

in which
A^{\sf s}
is as provided by
theorem

3.55
.
By
theorem

3.55
,
A^{\sf s}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
,

{\sf iK4}\vdash A^{\sf s}\to A
and

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf s}
.
Then
theorem

3.58
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ A^{\sf s}
.
Hence
theorem

2.16
implies
desired result.
∎

Theorem 3.57
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}
is sound and complete for
{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
-preservativity in

{\sf T}
, i.e. for every
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B.

Proof.

The left-to-right direction (soundness)
holds by
theorem

3.58
.

For the other direction (completeness), let

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
.
Then
theorems

3.56
and
2.11
implies

{\sf T}\vdash A^{\sf s}\to B
and hence

[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A^{\sf s}\rhd B
. Also by
theorem

3.55

we have
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}\vdash A\rhd A^{\sf s}
and
cut implies desired result.
∎

Theorem 3.58

(
Soundness
)
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
.

Proof.

Let
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}\vdash A\rhd B
.
Theorem

2.12
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ B
and thus
section

3.1
implies

A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ B
.
∎

For the uniformity of notations in the following corollary we consider
[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}
as the binary relation
which it axiomatizes.

Corollary 3.59
.

(
TYPE-
\alpha
)
{[\![{{\sf T},{\sf parb}}]\!]{\sf Le}{\sf A}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \ }={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\text{{\sf SPN}}(\Box)}^{\!\vee}}}$}\end{picture}}\quad\ \ }
. Moreover
If
{\sf T}
is decidable, then
all mentioned relations are decidable.

Proof.

All equalities are derived by

theorems

3.57
,
3.1
and
2.15
.
For the decidability of
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \
we have following argument.

Theorem

3.56
implies that

\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}}}
exists and is computable.
Then by
section

2.11
it is enough to decide

{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}}}\to B
, which is provided by decidability of
{\sf T}
.
∎

4
Provability and preservativity semantics

[
Iemhoff, 2001c
;
Iemhoff, 2001a
;
Iemhoff, 2003
]

consider some Kripke
semantic for the preservativity logic
{\sf{iPH}}
and proves its
soundness/completeness theorems.
Although such Kripke semantics are interesting tools,
the main obstacle for their usage is that they are infinite.
Here we introduce another semantics
for provability and preservativity logics.
This variant, as we will see later, enjoys
the finite model property which is the key point
for proving arithmetical completeness for the provability logic of

HA
and its decidability. The idea behind
provability and preservativity semantics is that it recursively assigns a theory
to every accessible node and defines validity of
\Box A

in this way: for every accessible node we have the
provability
of
A
in the assigned theory.
Since the assigned theory is defined via recurse on the accessibility relationship, we must require that the accessibility relation is conversely well-founded.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
{\sf T}
be a modal logic
and
\Delta\subseteq\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
sets of propositions such that
\Delta
and

\Gamma
are closed under
\Delta
-conjunctions, i.e.
A\in\Gamma

(
A\in\Delta
)
and
B\in\Delta
implies
A\wedge B\in\Gamma

(
A\wedge B\in\Delta
)
.
A quintuple
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)

is called a
(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantic if

\tilde{\mathcal{K}}:=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
is a transitive
conversely well-founded
Kripke model for the intuitionistic modal logic

(
as defined in
section

2.6
)
and

•

\varphi
is a function with the domain

W{\sqsubset}
, i.e. the set of all
\sqsubset
-accessible nodes in
W
.

•

\varphi(w)\in\Gamma
.
For the sake of simplicity of notations
we may write
\varphi_{w}
for
\varphi(w)
.

•

\mathcal{K}
satisfies compatibility, i.e. for
every
u\in W{\sqsubset}
we have

\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\varphi_{u}
, in which

\Vdash
is the forcing relation that will be defined below.

The forcing relation
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
is
defined by induction on
A

as we have in intuitionistic non-modal language,
and when
A=B\rhd C
is defined as follows:

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B\rhd C\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\forall u\sqsupset w\ \forall E\in\Delta\ (\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}E\to B\text{ implies }\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}E\to C),

in which

\Phi_{u}:=\{\varphi_{u}\}\cup\Delta^{\mathcal{K}}_{u}
and

\Delta^{\mathcal{K}}_{u}
is defined in the following line.
Given a set of modal propositions
Y
, define

Y^{\mathcal{K}}_{w}:=\{E\in Y:\mathcal{K},w\Vdash E\}\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{Y}^{\mathcal{K}}_{w}:=\{E\in Y:\mathcal{K},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}E\}.

For the sake of simplicity, whenever no confusion is likely we may omit the superscript
\mathcal{K}
and simply write
Y_{w}
and

\bar{Y}_{w}
instead.
Also the notation
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}A
is a shorthand for

“there is some
u\sqsubset w
such that
\mathcal{K},w^{\prime}\Vdash A
for every
w^{\prime}\sqsupset u
.”

We also define the following properties for preservativity semantics:

•

\mathcal{K}
is called
Y
-full if
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}Y_{u}
for every
u\in W{\sqsubset}
.

\mathcal{K}
is called full if it is
\Gamma
-full.
We say that
\mathcal{K}
is
A
-full if it is

Y
-full for
Y:=\{B,C:B\rhd C\in{\sf sub}(A)\}
.

•

\mathcal{K}
is called weakly
Y
-full if
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\bar{Y}_{u}
for every
u\in W{\sqsubset}
. Note that
since
\bar{Y}_{u}\subseteq Y_{u}
, weakly
Y
-fullness is implied from

Y
-fullness.

\mathcal{K}
is called weakly full
if it is weakly
\Gamma
-full.
We say that
\mathcal{K}
is weakly
A
-full if it is weakly

Y
-full for
Y:=\{B:\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)\}
.

•

We say that
\mathcal{K}
has a property of
intuitionistic modal Kripke models

(
like transitive
)
if
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}
is so.

Whenever
\Gamma=\Delta
we simply say that

\mathcal{K}
is a
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantic.
Note that in this case we may assume that

\varphi_{w}=\top
for every
w\in W{\sqsubset}
. Whenever
the triple
(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
may be inferred from context or we do not care what they are, we may simply say that
\mathcal{K}
is a preservativity semantic.

Remark 4.2
.

Since non-modal connectives of propositional language are
treated as usual in Kripke models of intuitionistic logic,
for every preservativity model
\mathcal{K}
we have
\mathcal{K}\Vdash{\sf IPC}
.

Remark 4.3
.

Whenever we restrict ourselves to the language
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
,
definition simplifies to following

(
remember that
\Box B=\top\rhd B
)
:

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\Box B\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\forall u\sqsupset w\ \Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B.

Whenever
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
is downward compact,
the preservativity semantics gets a more elegant form:

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
be downward compact,
\mathcal{K}:=(W,\succcurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
an
A
-full

(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantic and
\mathcal{K}\Vdash{\sf T}
.
Then for every
w\in W
and
\Box C\in{\sf sub}(A)
we have

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\Box C\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\forall u\sqsupset w\ \mathcal{K},u\Vdash\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}.

Proof.

Left-to-right:
Let
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\Box C
and
u\sqsupset w
seeking to show

\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.
By
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\Box C

we get
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}C
and then by conjunctive closure,
there is some
E\in\Phi_{u}\cap\Gamma

such that
{\sf T}\vdash E\to C
.
Then
{\sf T}\vdash E\to C

and
C\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
implies

{\sf T}\vdash E\to\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.
Since
{\sf T}
is sound for
\mathcal{K}
,
compatibility condition implies

\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.

Right-to-left: Let
u\sqsupset w

and
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
seeking to show

\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}C
.
From
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
and
soundness of
{\sf T}
we get
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash C
.
Then
A
-fullness implies
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}C
.
∎

The following theorem states that if we have some requirements, preservativity semantics coincides
with usual Kripke semantics for intuitionistic modal logics.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
and

\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
be weakly
A
-full

(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantic and
\mathcal{K}\Vdash{\sf T}
.
Then
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B

iff
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash B
for every

B\in{\sf sub}(A)
and
w\in W
.

Proof.

We use induction on
B\in{\sf sub}(A)
and show:

\mathcal{K},u\Vdash B\quad\text{ iff }\quad\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash B.

Hence by induction hypothesis, for

C\in{\sf sub}(B)
with
C\neq B
, and every
v\in W
we have

\mathcal{K},v\Vdash C\quad\text{ iff }\quad\tilde{\mathcal{K}},v\Vdash C.

If
B\in{\sf atom}
, obviously we are done. Also if
B=C\circ D
with

\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge,\to\}
one may use
induction hypothesis to prove desired result.
So it remains only
B=\Box C
.
First assume that
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\Box C

and hence for every

v\sqsupset u
we have
\Phi_{v}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}C
.
Then by soundness of
{\sf T}
and compatibility condition
we have
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash C

and hence by induction hypothesis

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},v\Vdash C
and thus

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash\Box C
.
For other direction around, let
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash\Box C
.
For arbitrary
v\sqsupset u
,
we have
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},v\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}C
and then by
induction hypothesis
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}C
.
Then by weak
A
-fullness we have

\Phi_{v}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}C
and
thus
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\Box C
.
∎

The forcing relationship for
finite Kripke models of
intuitionistic (modal) logic are obviously decidable,
i.e.
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
is decidable.
The following theorem shows that such decidability holds also for
some preservativity semantics.

Theorem 4.6
.

Forcing relationship for
finite
(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantic is decidable whenever

(\Delta,{\sf T})
is
recursively downward compact.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},X)
be a

(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantic.
We show decidability of
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
by double
induction first on
w

ordered by
\sqsupset
and second on
A
.
So as first induction hypothesis assume that for every

u\sqsupset w
and every
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

we have decidability of
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash B
.
Also as second induction hypothesis assume that for every

u\succcurlyeq w
we have decidability of
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash B
for every

B
which is strict subformula of
A
.
In the following cases for
A
, we show decidability of
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
:

•

A
is atomic. Obvious.

•

A
is a conjunction, disjunction or implication.
Use the second induction hypothesis.

•

A=\Box B
. It is enough to decide

\Delta_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\varphi_{u}\to B
for every
u\sqsupset w
.
Since
(\Delta,{\sf T})
is recursively downward compact,
one may effectively compute
\lfloor{\varphi_{u}\to B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Delta}}}
.
By definition of
\lfloor{.}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
it is enough to decide

\Delta_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\lfloor{\varphi_{u}\to B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Delta}}}

which is equivalent to

\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\lfloor{\varphi_{u}\to B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Delta}}}
.
Now the first induction hypothesis implies decidability of

\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\lfloor{\varphi_{u}\to B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Delta}}}
.∎

As one expect, preservativity relation
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}

must be sound for preservativity semantics:

Lemma 4.7
.

\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
is sound for
(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantics,
i.e. given such preservativity semantics
\mathcal{K}
,
we have
\mathcal{K}\Vdash A\rhd B
whenever
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
.

Proof.

Let
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
and

\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
be a

(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantics and
w\sqsubset u\in W

and
E\in\Delta

such that
\Phi_{u},E\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A
.
Hence there is a finite set

\Phi^{\prime}_{u}\subseteq\Phi_{u}\cap\Delta

such that
\Phi^{\prime}_{u},E,\varphi_{u}\vdash A
. By conjunctive closure condition, we have
\bigwedge\Phi_{u}\wedge E\wedge\varphi_{u}\in\Gamma
and thus by
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
we get

\Phi_{u}^{\prime},E,\varphi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
. Hence we have

\Phi_{u},E\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
.
∎

4.1
General soundness and completeness

In the current subsection we prove general soundness and completeness theorems of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})
for preservativity semantics.

Theorem 4.8

(
Soundness
)
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})
is sound for

(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantics
whenever
{\sf IPC}\subseteq{\sf T}
and
{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\subseteq\Delta
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
is a

(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantics.
We use induction on
w\in W
ordered by

\sqsupset
and show
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})
.
We also use another induction on
the proof
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})\vdash A
and show

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
.
Remember that (
section

2.4
)

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})
’s only inference rule is modus ponens and instead of necessitation we added
\Box A
for every axiom instance of

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})
.

•

A=(\Box(B\to C)\wedge\Box B)\to\Box C
.
Let
v\succcurlyeq w
such that
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash\Box(B\to C)

and
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash\Box B
. Given

u\sqsupset v
, we must show

\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}C
. By
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash\Box(B\to C)

and
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash\Box B
we have

\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B\to C

and
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
.
Thus
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}C
.

•

A=\Box B\to\Box\Box B
. Let
v\succcurlyeq w
such that

\mathcal{K},v\Vdash\Box B
and assume that
u\sqsupset v
.
It is enough to show

\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\Box B
. For every
u^{\prime}\sqsupset u

by transitivity
we have
u^{\prime}\sqsupset v
and hence
by
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash\Box B
we get
\Phi_{u^{\prime}}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
.
Thus
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\Box B
and since

\Box B\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\subseteq\Delta

we have
\Box B\in\Phi_{u}
.

•

A=p\to\Box p
for
p\in{\sf par}
. Let
v\succcurlyeq w
such that
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash p
and
u\sqsupset v

seeking to show
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}p
.
Since
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash p

we get
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash p
and
since
p\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\subseteq\Delta
we have

p\in\Phi_{u}
.

•

A=\Box(\Box B\to B)\to\Box B
.
Let
v\succcurlyeq w
such that

\mathcal{K},v\Vdash\Box(\Box B\to B)

and assume that
u\sqsupset v
seeking to show

\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
. Since
u\sqsupset v
, transitivity implies
that also we have
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\Box(\Box B\to B)
.
First induction hypothesis implies

\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\Box(\Box B\to B)\to\Box B
.
Hence
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\Box B
and since
\Box B\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\subseteq\Delta

we get
\Box B\in\Phi_{u}
.
Since
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash\Box(\Box B\to B)

we have
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\Box B\to B

and thus
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
.

•

A=\Box B\to\Box C
and

B\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}C
.
Section

4
.

•

A=\Box B
and
B
is an instance of above axioms.
Let
u\sqsupset w
seeking to show

\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
.
By the first induction hypothesis
we have
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B

and
since
\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\subseteq\Delta
we have

\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}B\in\Phi_{u}
and hence
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
.

•

A
is an
{\sf IPC}
-valid theorem.
Section

4
.

•

A=\Box B
and
B
is an
{\sf IPC}
-valid theorem.
Let
u\sqsupset w
. Then since

{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf IPC}
we have
{\sf T}\vdash B

and hence
\Phi_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
.

•

A
is derived by modus ponens from
B
and

B\to A
.
Use the second induction hypothesis.∎

Theorem 4.9

(
Completeness
)
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})
is complete for good

(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-semantics, if

(\Gamma,{\sf T})
is strong downward compact and

\Gamma\supseteq{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
is closed under conjunction
and
{\sf T}\supseteq{\sf IPC}
.

Proof.

Let
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})\nvdash A
. Then by
section

2.11
we also have
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})\nvdash\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}
and hence

{\sf iGL}\nvdash\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}
.
Theorem

2.2

implies that there is some good Kripke model

\tilde{\mathcal{K}}:=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
such that

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w_{0}\nVdash\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}
.
Define
\varphi(w):=\top
for the preservativity semantic

\mathcal{K}:=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
.
The definition of
X
is such that evidently

\mathcal{K}
is compatible.

Theorem

4.5

implies
\mathcal{K},w_{0}\nVdash\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}
and

theorem

4.8
implies

\mathcal{K}\Vdash{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})
. By
section

2.11
we have

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}({\Gamma},{{\sf T}})\vdash A\leftrightarrow\lfloor\!\!\lfloor{A}\rfloor\!\!\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\Gamma}}
and thus

\mathcal{K},w_{0}\nVdash A
.
∎

Corollary 4.10
.

{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}

(
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}
)
is sound and complete for
(
{\sf C_{a}}
)
good

({\sf S}{\sf N},{\sf iGLC_{a}})
-semantics.

Proof.

Theorems

4.9
,
4.8
and
3.12

∎

Corollary 4.11
.

{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}
is sound and complete for good

({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf iGL})
-semantics.

Proof.

Theorems

4.9
,
4.8
and
3.56

∎

Although it is pleasing to have such a good general soundness and completeness for several logics, there are limitations: we are not able to apply this
and have completeness result
for
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}:={\sf iGL}{\sf H}({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}},{{\sf iGL}})
.
The major obstacle for doing so is
that
{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
is not closed under conjunction.
However we have the soundness result for
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
:

Corollary 4.12
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
is sound for

({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantics.

Proof.

Theorem

4.8
.
∎

4.2
Preservativity semantics for
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}

In this section, we prove completeness of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}

for good
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantics. This completeness
result will be helpful in proving “
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
implies

{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\nvdash\gamma(A)
” (
theorem

5.1
) which itself implies the arithmetical completeness of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
.
We also will use
the completeness of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
for preservativity semantics in

section

4.3
to provide decidability of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
.

We say that
Y\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
is
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
-adequate if

•

\bot\in Y
and

Y
is closed under subformulas.

•

If
A,B\in Y
then
\lfloor{A\wedge B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!\Gamma}}_{{}_{\!{{\sf T}}}}\in Y
.
More precisely, it means that for every
A,B\in Y

there is some

C\in Y\cap\Gamma

such that
{\sf T}\vdash C\to(A\wedge B)

and for every
E\in\Gamma

with
{\sf T}\vdash E\to(A\wedge B)
we have
{\sf T}\vdash E\to C
.

Also a set
\Delta
is called
Y
-saturated w.r.t.
{\sf T}
iff

•

\Delta\subseteq Y
,

•

if
\Delta\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
and
B\in Y
then

B\in\Delta
,

•

\Delta\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\bot
,

•

B\vee C\in\Delta
implies either
B\in\Delta
or
C\in\Delta
.

Theorem 4.13

(
Completeness
)
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
is complete for
good
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantics.

Proof.

Let
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
.
We construct a Kripke model

\tilde{\mathcal{K}}:=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V})
as follows.
By
section

4.2
there is some finite
set
Y\ni A
which is
({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf iGL})
-adequate.
Let
Y^{\prime}:=Y\cup\{\Box B:B\in Y\}
and define:

•

W:=
the set of all
Y^{\prime}
-saturated sets
w.r.t.
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
.

•

{\preccurlyeq}:={\subseteq}
.

•

w\sqsubset u
iff

–

B,\Box B\in u
,

–

there is some
\Box E\in u\setminus w
.

•

w\mathrel{V}a
iff
a\in w
for atomic
a
and
w\in W
.

Before we continue with definition of
\mathcal{K}
,
observe that

\tilde{\mathcal{K}}
is a Kripke model for intuitionistic modal logic
and is finite irreflexive transitive
{\sf C_{p}}
model.
Next we show that
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}\nVdash A
. Since
{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}\nvdash A
,

section

4.2
implies that there is some set
u

which is
Y^{\prime}
-saturated w.r.t.
{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}
and
A\not\in u
. Thus
by
section

4.2
we have
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\nVdash A
.
Finally we define
\mathcal{K}:=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)

in which
\varphi_{w}
is defined for every
w\in{\sqsubset}W
as follows.

\varphi^{\prime}_{w}:=\bigwedge({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}\cap\bar{Y}_{w})\quad\text{in which}\quad\bar{Y}_{w}:=\{B\in Y:\mathcal{K},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}B\}

We will see later in this proof that
\varphi^{\prime}_{w}
as defined above
belongs to
{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
.
Hence (modulo
{\sf iGL}
-provable equivalence relation)

\varphi^{\prime}_{w}=\bigvee\Gamma_{w}
for some finite set
\Gamma_{w}\subseteq{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
. Define

\varphi_{w}\in\Gamma_{w}
such that
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\varphi_{w}
.

We will see in the rest of this proof that

\mathcal{K}
as defined above is a weakly

Y
-full
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic and hence by

theorem

4.5
we get
\mathcal{K}\nVdash A
.
Then
section

4.2
implies desired completeness.
So it remains only to prove by induction on
W
that:
(1)
\varphi^{\prime}_{w}\in{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
(which implies that
\mathcal{K}
as defined above is an
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic indeed),
(2)
\Phi_{w}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}}$}\end{picture}}\bar{Y}_{w}
. The induction will be done on the
\sqsubset
-height of
w\in W
. The height
h(w)
of a node
w\in W

is defined as the maximum
n
such that the following sequence exists:

w_{0}\sqsubset w_{1}\sqsubset w_{2}\sqsubset\ldots\sqsubset w_{n}.

So as induction hypothesis assume that for every

v\in W
with
h(v)>h(w)
we have
(1)
\varphi^{\prime}_{v}\in{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
,
(2)
\Phi_{v}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}}$}\end{picture}}\bar{Y}_{v}
.
Consider the
Kripke model
\mathcal{K}_{v}
as restriction of
\mathcal{K}

to the nodes which are accessible (via transitive closure
of
{\preccurlyeq}\cup{\sqsubset}
)
from
v
.
Obviously
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash B
iff
\mathcal{K}_{v},u\Vdash B

for every accessible node
u
from
v
and every
B
.
It is not difficult to observe that for every
v\in W
with

h(v)\geq h(w)

induction hypothesis implies

\mathcal{K}_{v}
is weakly
Y
-full
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic.
Hence by
theorem

4.5

for every
B\in Y
and
u
in
\mathcal{K}_{v}
we have

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash B
iff
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash B
. Thus

(*)  for every
B\in Y
and every
v
with

h(v)\geq h(w)
we have

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},v\Vdash B
iff
\mathcal{K},v\Vdash B
.

In the rest of this proof assume that

w\in W
is a
\sqsubset
-accessible node.

•

\varphi^{\prime}_{w}\in{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
. We first show that
if
E,F\in\bar{Y}_{w}\cap{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
then there is some

G\in\bar{Y}_{w}\cap{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}

such that
{\sf iGL}\vdash G\to(E\wedge F)
. Then since
Y
is finite,
there must be a single
G\in\bar{Y}_{w}\cap{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
such that

G\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}}$}\end{picture}}\bar{Y}_{w}\cap{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
and hence

G
is equivalent to
\varphi^{\prime}_{w}
.
Thus
\varphi^{\prime}_{w}\in{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
, as required.
So assume that
E,F\in\bar{Y}_{w}\cap{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
.
Then (*) implies
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}E,F
.
By
({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf iGL})
-adequacy of
Y
, we have

\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in Y
. Let
w^{\prime}\in W

be such that
w^{\prime}\sqsubset w
and
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w^{\prime}\Vdash\Box E\wedge\Box F
.

Section

4.2
implies
\Box E,\Box F\in w^{\prime}
.
Since

{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}\vdash\Box(E\wedge F)\to\Box\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
,
we get
\Box\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in w^{\prime}

and hence
section

4.2
implies

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
.
Thus (*) implies
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
and hence

\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in\bar{Y}_{w}\cap{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}
and

{\sf iGL}\vdash\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\to(E\wedge F)
.

•

Weakly
Y
-fullness.
Let
B\in Y

such that
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}B
seeking to show

\varphi_{w}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}}$}\end{picture}}B
. Since
\vdash\varphi_{w}\to\varphi^{\prime}_{w}
,
it is enough to show
\varphi^{\prime}_{w}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}}$}\end{picture}}B
.
Since
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}B
,
by the statement (*) we have
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}B
.
By definition of
Y

we have
\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in Y
.
Let
w^{\prime}\in W
be such that
w^{\prime}\sqsubset w
and
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w^{\prime}\Vdash\Box B
.
Then
section

4.2
implies
\Box B\in w^{\prime}
.
Since
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash\Box B\to\Box\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
we get

\Box\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in w^{\prime}
and again
section

4.2
implies

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash^{{}^{\!\!+}}\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
.
Since
\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}

we get
\vdash\varphi^{\prime}_{w}\to\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
and hence

{\sf iGL}\vdash\varphi^{\prime}_{w}\to B
.
∎

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
be a finite transitive
irreflexive
(\Delta,\Gamma,{\sf T})
-model.
Then for every
w_{0}\in W

there is some good Kripke model

\mathcal{T}=(W^{\prime},\preccurlyeq^{\prime},\sqsubset^{\prime},\mathrel{V}^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})

with the root
w^{\prime}_{0}
,
which is equivalent to
\mathcal{K}
, i.e. there is a function
e:W^{\prime}\longrightarrow W
such that

e(w^{\prime}_{0})=w_{0}
and for every

w^{\prime}\in W^{\prime}
and
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
we have
\mathcal{T},w^{\prime}\Vdash A

iff
\mathcal{K},e(w^{\prime})\Vdash A
.

Proof.

First define
(W^{\prime},\preccurlyeq^{\prime},\sqsubset^{\prime},\mathrel{V}^{\prime})
and the function
e
same
as in the proof of
section

2.6
. Then let

\varphi^{\prime}(w^{\prime}):=\varphi(e(w^{\prime}))
.
The rest of proof is straightforward and left to the reader.
∎

Lemma 4.15

(
Truth
)
.

Let
\tilde{\mathcal{K}}
and
Y^{\prime}
as defined in the proof of

theorem

4.13
. Then

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash B
iff
B\in w
for every
B\in Y^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We use induction on
B\in Y^{\prime}
.

•

B\in{\sf atom}
. Then by definition we have

B\in w
iff
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash B
.

•

B=E\wedge F
. Since
Y^{\prime}
is closed under subformulas
and
w
is closed under derivability of propositions in
Y^{\prime}
,
we have
E\wedge F\in w
iff
E,F\in w
. Thus induction hypothesis
implies desired result.

•

B=E\vee F
. First let
B\in w
. Since

w\in W
, it is
Y^{\prime}
-saturated w.r.t.
{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}
and hence
either
E\in w
or
F\in w
. Thus by induction hypothesis (and closure of
Y
under subformulas) we get either

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash E
or
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash F
. In either of the cases
we have
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash E\vee F
. For the other way around, let

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash E\vee F
. Then either
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash E
or

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash F
. Hence induction hypothesis
implies
E\in w
or
F\in w
. Since

{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}\vdash E\to(E\vee F)
and
{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}\vdash F\to(E\vee F)

we have
E\vee F\in w
.

•

B=E\to F
. First assume that
E\to F\in w
and
u\succcurlyeq w

and
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash E
seeking to show
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash F
.
Induction hypothesis implies
E\in u
.
Then since
u\supseteq w
we have
E\to F\in u

and thus
F\in u
. Again by induction hypothesis
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash F
.

For other way around,
let
E\to F\not\in w
.
Thus
w,E\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ F
and by

section

4.2
we get some
u\supseteq w\cup\{E\}

which is
Y^{\prime}
-saturaetd w.r.t.
{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}
and

u\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ F
.
Thus
u\in W
and
F\not\in u
and by induction hypothesis
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\nVdash F

and
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash E
. Hence
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},w\nVdash E\to F
.

•

B=\Box C
. First assume that
\Box C\in w
and

u\sqsupset w
seeking to show

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash C
.
Since
\Box C\in w
, by definition of
w\sqsubset u
, we get

C\in u
and thus by induction hypothesis
\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash C
.

For other way around, let

\Box C\not\in w
seeking some
u\sqsupset w
such that

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\nVdash C
.
Since
\Box C\not\in w
we have

w\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \Box C
.
Let us define

w_{\Box}:=\{E,\Box E:\Box E\in w\}
and

u_{0}:=w_{\Box}\cup\{\Box C\}
.
If
u_{0}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ C
then

w_{\Box}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \Box C\to C
and hence

\Box w_{\Box}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \Box(\Box C\to C)
.
Since
w\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \Box w_{\Box}
we get

w\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \ \Box(\Box C\to C)
.
Then by Löb’s principle we have

w\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \Box C
, a contradiction.
Hence we have

u_{0}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ C
, and
by
section

4.2
there is some

u\supseteq u_{0}
which is
Y^{\prime}
-saturated w.r.t.
{{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}

and
u\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGL}{\sf H}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ C
.
Hence
C\not\in u
and
w\sqsubset u
.
Thus induction hypothesis implies

\tilde{\mathcal{K}},u\nVdash C
.
∎

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
Y
be a
set of propositions closed under subformula and

u_{0}\subseteq Y
a set
such that
u_{0}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A
.
Then there is some

u\supseteq u_{0}
which is
Y
-saturated
w.r.t.
{\sf T}
and
u\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A
.

Proof.

Any maximal (with respect to
\subseteq
) set
u
of propositions
with the following properties is
Y
-saturated with respect to
{\sf T}
:

•

u_{0}\subseteq u\subseteq Y
,

•

u\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A
. ∎

Lemma 4.17
.

For every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
,
there is a finite

(
modulo
{\sf iK4}
-provable equivalence relation
)

set
Y\ni A
which is
({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf iGL})
-adequate.
Moreover one may effectively compute such finite set.

Proof.

We only show the existence of
Y
and leave its computability to the reader.
First we define
Y_{0}
and
Y_{1}
and then define

Y^{\prime}:=Y_{0}\cup Y_{1}\cup\{B\wedge C:B\in Y_{0},C\in Y_{1}\}\quad\text{ and }\quad Y:=\{\bigvee X:X\subseteq_{\text{fin}}Y^{\prime}\}.

Y_{0}
:

Let
X:={\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}}(A)
and define

Y_{0}:=\{B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}(X):c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B)\leq c^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)\}.

Remember that
c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}
counts the number of nested implications which are outside boxes and

c^{\Box}_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(A)
is defined as maximum of

|{\sf sub^{{\sf atomb}}}(A)|

and
\max\{c_{\!{}_{\to}\!}(B):\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)\}
.

Section

2.5

implies that
Y_{0}
is finite
up to
{\sf IPC}
-provable equivalence relation.

Y_{1}
:

Let
Z:={\sf sub^{{\sf parb}}}(A)\cup\{\Box B:B\in Y_{0}\}
.
Then by
section

2.9
the set

Y^{\prime}_{1}:={{\sf NNIL}(Z)}

is finite up to
{\sf iK4}
-provable equivalence relationship.
Then define
Y_{1}:=\{B^{\Box}:B\in Y^{\prime}_{1}\}
.

We show that
Y
is
({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf iGL})
-adequate.
Since
Y_{0}
and
Y_{1}
are closed under subformulas,
Y

is also closed under subformulas. Given
B,C\in Y^{\prime}

we show that a glb for
B\wedge C
w.r.t.
({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},\Gamma)

belongs to
Y
. Assume that

B=E\wedge E^{\prime}
and
C=F\wedge F^{\prime}
with

E,F\in Y_{0}
and
E^{\prime},F^{\prime}\in Y_{1}
.
Since
E^{\prime},F^{\prime}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
,
we have
\lfloor{B\wedge C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}=\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\wedge(E^{\prime}\wedge F^{\prime})
.
It is enough to show
\lfloor{E\wedge F}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in Y
.
Since
E\wedge F\in Y_{0}
,
section

4.2
implies desired result.
∎

Lemma 4.18
.

B\in Y_{0}
implies
\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf iGL}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}\in Y
.

Proof.

Let
\Pi^{1}_{B}

as defined in
section

3.4
.

Sections

3.4
and
3.4

imply
\Pi^{1}_{B}\subseteq Y_{0}
.
On the other hand
theorem

3.25
implies that

\bigvee\Pi^{1}_{B}

is a glb for
B
w.r.t.
({\downarrow}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee},{\sf iGL})
.

Section

3.6
implies that
\bigvee\hat{\Pi}^{1}_{B}

is a glb for
B
w.r.t.
({{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}},{\sf iGL})
, i.e.
\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf iGL}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}=\bigvee\hat{\Pi}^{1}_{B}
.
As defined in the statement of
section

3.6

we have

\hat{\Pi}^{1}_{B}:=\{D\wedge\Box D\wedge(D^{\dagger})^{\Box}:D\in\Pi^{1}_{B}\}
and thus
\lfloor{B}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf iGL}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}^{\!\vee}}}}}\in Y
.
∎

4.3
Decidability of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}

In this section we show that
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
is decidable.
The idea behind the proof of decidability is to use finite model property that we have from
theorem

4.13
.

So let us start with the gist:
given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
we must decide
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A
.
First compute
n_{A}
and
\Gamma_{A}
,
as provided by
section

4.3
.
Then
check the validity of
A
in (root of) all good

({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantics
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)

with less than
n_{A}
nodes and
\varphi(w)\in\Gamma_{A}
for every

\sqsubset
-accessible
w\in W
.

Theorems

4.6
and
3.56
together with
decidability of
{\sf iGL}
imply that
the validity in
\mathcal{K}
is decidable. If all such models satisfy

A
return
yes
, otherwise
no
.
Thus we have following decidability result:

Theorem 4.19
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
is decidable.

Lemma 4.20
.

Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
with
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
,
there is some
n_{A}\in\mathbb{N}
and a finite set

\Gamma_{A}\subseteq{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
such that:

•

n_{A}
and
\Gamma_{A}
are effectively computable,

•

there is some
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic

\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
with

\mathcal{K}\nVdash A
and
|W|\leq n_{A}
and
\varphi(w)\in\Gamma_{A}

for every
\sqsubset
-accessible
w\in W
.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma_{A}:=Y_{A}\cap{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
, in which
Y_{A}
is as provided by

section

4.2
. The number
n_{A}
and

\mathcal{K}
can be easily inferred from the proof of
theorem

4.13
.
∎

5
Provability logic of
HA
: arithmetical completeness

In this section we prove that
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
(see
section

4.2
) is the provability logic of
HA
. We already have the soundness
by
sections

3.6
and
2.20
. So it remains to prove arithmetical
completeness, which is proved by propositional reduction to
the completeness of
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}
for
\Sigma_{1}
-substitutions.

Historically, the Provability Logic of Peano Arithmetic,
PA
is discovered
[
Solovay, 1976
]
before the
\Sigma_{1}
-Provability
Logic of
PA

[
Visser, 1981
]
. The method used in
[
Visser, 1981
]

essentially uses Solovay’s technique in
[
Visser, 1981
]
. Then

[
Ardeshir and Mojtahedi, 2015
]
shows that in a sense, the
\Sigma_{1}
-provability
logic of
PA
is harder than the standard provability logic of
PA
.
Later
[
Mojtahedi, 2021
]
studies
reductions

between provability logics and characterizes several other provability logics. Most notably, it is shown in

[
Mojtahedi, 2021
]

that the
\Sigma_{1}
-provability logic of
HA

relative in the standard model, is the hardest known provability logic.
Here in this section (see
theorem

5.1
) we show that the

\Sigma_{1}
-provability logic of
HA

[
Ardeshir and Mojtahedi, 2018
;
Visser and Zoethout, 2019
]
is harder than
the standard provability logic of
HA
, or in other words, we
reduce “completeness of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
for arithmetical interpretations in
HA
” to the “completeness if
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}
for arithmetical
\Sigma_{1}

interpretations in
HA
”.

Theorem 5.1

(
Reduction
)
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
implies

{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\nvdash\theta(A)
for some substitution
\theta
.

Proof.

Let
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
.

Theorem

5.4
gives us some
substitution
\gamma
such that

{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash\gamma(A)
and hence by
theorem

5.18

we have
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\nvdash\beta(\gamma(A))
for some substitution

\beta
. Then if we let
\theta:=\beta\circ\gamma
we are done.
∎

Corollary 5.2

(
Arithmetical Completeness
)
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
is complete for arithmetical interpretations in
HA
,
i.e. if for every arithmetical substitution
\alpha
we have

\hbox{\sf HA}{}\vdash\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(A)
then
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A
.

Proof.

We reason contrapositively. So assume that
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A

seeking some arithmetical substitution
\alpha
such that

\hbox{\sf HA}{}\nvdash\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(A)
.
By
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
and
theorem

5.1
we get
some propositional substitution

\theta
such that
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\nvdash\theta(A)
.
Then by
theorem

2.22
there is some
arithmetical substitution
\sigma
such that

\hbox{\sf HA}{}\nvdash\sigma_{{}_{\sf HA}}(\theta(A))
. Thus

\alpha:=\sigma\circ\theta
finishes the proof.
∎

Recall definitions of
{\sf{iPH}}
and
{\sf{iPH}}^{+}
from
section

2.12

and define
{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}
(
{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}^{+}
) as the
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
-fragment of

{\sf{iPH}}
(
{\sf{iPH}}^{+}
). As a corollary to arithmetical completeness, we have:

Corollary 5.3
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}={\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}={\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}^{+}
.

Proof.

We show
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\subseteq{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}\subseteq{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}^{+}\subseteq{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
.

•

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\subseteq{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}
:
Section

3.6
.

•

{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}\subseteq{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}^{+}
: Obvious.

•

{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}^{+}\subseteq{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
:
We reason contrapositively. Assume that
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
.
Then
section

5
implies
\hbox{\sf HA}{}\nvdash\alpha_{{}_{\sf HA}}(A)
.
Thus
theorem

2.20
implies
{\sf{iPH}}^{+}\nvdash A
. ∎

The proof of above corollary uses
arithmetical soundness and completeness theorems.
However the statement of

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}={\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}={\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}^{+}
tempts us for finding a propositional proof of this fact without using arithmetical interpretations.
So we pose the following question:

Question 3
.

Is there a translation
(.)^{\sf t}:\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}\to\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

with the following properties:

•

{\sf{iPH}}^{+}\vdash A
implies
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A^{\sf t}
for every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\rhd}
.

•

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A\leftrightarrow A^{\sf t}
for every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
.

Note that if such translation exists, one may easily prove

section

5
without using the arithmetical
soundness-completeness theorems:

Proof of
section

5
.

The proof of
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\subseteq{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}\subseteq{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}^{+}
goes as before.
For
{\sf{iPH}}_{\Box}^{+}\subseteq{\sf iGL}{\sf H}
we have following argument.
Assume that
{\sf{iPH}}^{+}\vdash A
for
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
.
Then
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A^{\sf t}
and since
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A\leftrightarrow A^{\sf t}

we get
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\vdash A
.
∎

5.1
First step reduction:
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
implies

{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash\gamma(A)

In this subsection we prove
theorem

5.4
:

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
implies
{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash\gamma(A)
.
All consequent technical lemmas are used only for the proof
of
theorem

5.4
. For the sake of simplicity of notations in the rest of this section we use

\vdash
for derivability in
{\sf iGL}
, unless we say otherwise.

For this reduction, we transfer

({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantics
to
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf iGL})
-semantics.
Before we dive into long details of this transformation,
let us explain some ideas behind it.
Let
\mathcal{K}\nVdash A
for some
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantics

\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
. Since
\varphi_{w}\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
,
there is some projective substitution
\theta_{w}
such that

\varphi_{w}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-4.74423pt\raisebox{6.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta_{w}$}}\hskip-4.74423pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip-5.06528pt\raisebox{-6.8611pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sf iGL}$}}\hskip-5.06528pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
. We use
these substitutions in a systematic way to produce our intended transformation. One obstacle in this way is that,
by definition of projectivity, we actually
are working with the outer substitution
\hat{\theta}_{w}

and not
\theta_{w}
itself.
This means that
\hat{\theta}_{w}
is identity on boxed formulas. We use the simultaneous fixed-point theorem in
{\sf iGL}
(see
section

2.13
) to simulate such condition.

Important notation:

Unless stated otherwise, in
sections

5.1
and
5.2
, we use

\vdash
for derivability in
{\sf iGL}
.

Theorem 5.4
.

{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
implies
{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash\gamma(A)

for some substitution
\gamma
.

Proof.

Let us first define some notions. Given a
preservativity semantic

\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
, we say that a node

w\in W
is
{\sf H}
-node,
if (1)
w
is
\sqsubset
-accessible and (2)

\varphi_{u}\neq\top
for some
u\sqsubseteq w
.
Also define a complexity measure
\mathfrak{d}(A)

whenever
{\sf iGL}{\sf H}\nvdash A
,
as follows.
\mathfrak{d}(A)

is the minimum number
d

such that there is some
good
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic

\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)
with
d
as the number of

{\sf H}
-nodes.
Note that by
theorem

4.13

there is some such preservativity semantic indeed.
Then we use induction on
\mathfrak{d}(A)
and prove the statement of this
lemma.

If
\mathfrak{d}(A)=0
, then
\mathcal{K}
is also
an
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf iGL})
-semantic and hence

section

4.1
implies
{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash A
. Thus in this case
we are done if we let
\gamma:=
identity substitution.
As induction hypothesis assume that for every

A^{\prime}
with
\mathfrak{d}(A^{\prime})<\mathfrak{d}(A)
we have
{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash\gamma(A^{\prime})

for some substitution
\gamma
.
Then by induction hypothesis, it is enough to find some substitution
\gamma
such that
\mathfrak{d}(\gamma(A))<\mathfrak{d}(A)
.
Let
\mathcal{K}^{0}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V}^{0},\varphi^{0})
be a good

({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic
with the root
w_{0}
such that
\mathcal{K}^{0},w_{0}\nVdash A

and the number of
{\sf H}
-nodes in
\mathcal{K}^{0}
is
\mathfrak{d}(A)
.
Moreover we assume that whenever
w\sqsubset u
, we have

\vdash\varphi^{0}_{u}\to\varphi^{0}_{w}
.
The reason for such assumption
follows. By compatibility condition,
we have
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\varphi^{0}_{w}
. Since
\varphi^{0}_{w}\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}

we have
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\Box\varphi^{0}_{w}
and thus

{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}_{u},\varphi^{0}_{u}\vdash\varphi^{0}_{w}
. Hence there is some

E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}_{u}
such that
\varphi^{0}_{u},E\vdash\varphi^{0}_{w}
. Note that
\varphi^{0}_{u}\wedge E\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}_{u}
.
Thus one may change the definition of
\varphi^{0}
accordingly to satisfy mentioned extra condition.

In the rest of this subsection we fix some

{\sf H}
-node
{w_{1}}\in W
which is
\sqsubset
-minimal,
i.e. for every
w^{\prime}\sqsubset w_{1}
is not
{\sf H}
-node.
Note that whenever
w
is not
{\sf H}
-node, we have

\varphi^{0}_{w}=\top
.

Let
W_{0}:=\{w_{0}\}
and
W_{i+1}:=\{w\in W:w\text{ is an immediate $\sqsubset$-successor of some }w^{\prime}\in W_{i}\}
. By an immediate
\sqsubset
-successor of
w\in W
we mean some
u\in W
such that
w\sqsubset u
and
there is no
v\in W
with
w\sqsubset v\sqsubset u
. Since
\mathcal{K}

is conversely well-founded, there is some maximum number

n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
W_{n}
is nonempty. We define

W^{\prime}:=\bigcup_{i=0}^{n}W_{i}
and
W^{\prime\prime}:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}
.
In the rest of this subsection, let
\chi:=\varphi^{0}_{w_{1}}
.
Since
\chi\in{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}}
,
there is some
\chi
-projective (in
{\sf iGL}
)
substitution
\theta
such that

\vdash\hat{\theta}(\chi)\leftrightarrow\chi^{\dagger}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
and

\vdash\chi\to(x\leftrightarrow\theta(x))
for every variable
x
,
and moreover
\theta(x)=x
for every

x\not\in{\sf sub}(A)
(by
section

3.2
).
Since for every
u\sqsupset w
we have

{\sf iGL}\vdash\varphi^{0}_{u}\to\varphi^{0}_{w}
,

{\sf iGL}\vdash\hat{\theta}(\chi)\leftrightarrow\chi^{\dagger}
and
{\sf iGL}\vdash\chi\to(x\leftrightarrow\theta(x))
, there is some finite set
Y\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})

of
{\sf iGL}
-axioms such that

Y\vdash_{\sf IPC}\varphi^{0}_{u}\to\varphi^{0}_{w}
,

Y\vdash_{\sf IPC}\hat{\theta}(\chi)\leftrightarrow\chi^{\dagger}
and

Y\vdash_{\sf IPC}\chi\to(a\leftrightarrow\theta(a))
for every

a\in{\sf atom}
and
w\sqsubset u
.
Let

{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern includes all boxed subformulas of elements in

Y\cup\{\chi\}
. Also let

{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern and

are fresh

or

.

, i.e.

for every

.
Moreover define

such that
\eta(Y^{\prime})=Y
and

\eta(\chi^{\ddagger})=\chi^{\dagger}
and

\eta(\chi^{\prime})=\chi
.
Then obviously we have

Y^{\prime}\vdash_{{\sf IPC}}\alpha(\chi^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\chi^{\ddagger}

and
Y^{\prime}\vdash_{\sf IPC}\chi^{\prime}\to(x\leftrightarrow\alpha(x))
for every

x\in{\sf var}
.
Define the following substitution:

\beta(a):=\begin{cases}(q\to\alpha(a))\wedge(\neg q\to a)\quad&:a\in{\sf var}\\
a&:a\in{\sf par}\cup\{\bot\}\end{cases}

Let
\tau
be the simulataneous fixed point
of
\beta(\Box B_{1}),\ldots,\beta(\Box B_{m})

with respect to

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle p\hfil

, as provided by

theorem

2.21
.
This means that
{\sf iGL}\vdash\tau(p_{i})\leftrightarrow\tau\beta(\Box B_{i})
.
Finally define
\gamma:=\tau\circ\beta
.

Then define
\mathcal{K}^{1}:=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V}^{1},\varphi^{0})
with

W,\preccurlyeq
and
\sqsubset
and
\varphi^{0}
as we had
in
\mathcal{K}^{0}
, and
\mathrel{V}^{1}

as follows:

w\mathrel{V}^{1}p_{i}\quad\text{iff}\quad\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash\Box B_{i},\quad\quad w\mathrel{V}^{1}q\quad\text{iff}\quad{w_{1}}\mathrel{\widetilde{\sqsubseteq}}w\quad\text{iff}\quad\exists\,v\ {w_{1}}\sqsubseteq v\preccurlyeq w,

and also define
w\mathrel{V}^{1}a
iff
w\mathrel{V}^{0}a
, for every other atomic
a
.
Note that in above definition, there is a recursion when we are defining
V^{1}
(and later when we are defining
V^{2}
)
and it is a valid definition
because
(W,\sqsubset)
is conversely well-founded.
One may easily observe by induction on
w
(ordered by
\sqsupset
)
that
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash B
iff
\mathcal{K}^{0},w\Vdash B
for every
B
that

p_{i}
’s and
q
not appeared.
Hence
\mathcal{K}^{1}
satisfies compatibility condition and

\mathcal{K}^{1},w_{0}\nVdash A
. Moreover
\mathcal{K}^{1}
is also a good

({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic
and
\mathcal{K}^{1}
and
\mathcal{K}^{0}
share the same set of

{\sf H}
-nodes.

Finally define
\mathcal{K}^{2}:=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V}^{2},\varphi^{2})
with

(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset)
as we had in
\mathcal{K}^{0}
and
(\mathrel{V}^{2},\varphi^{2})
as follows:

\varphi^{2}_{w}:=\begin{cases}\varphi^{0}_{w}\quad&:w\neq{w_{1}}\\
\top&:w={w_{1}}\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\begin{cases}w\mathrel{V}^{2}p_{i}\text{ iff }\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\Box\gamma(B_{i})\\
w\mathrel{V}^{2}a\text{ iff }w\mathrel{V}^{1}a\text{ for every other atomic }a\end{cases}

Also define

{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{i}_{w}:=\{E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}:\mathcal{K}^{i},w\Vdash E\}
for

i\in\{0,1,2\}
.
For our later applications, let us also define:

\psi:=\bigwedge\{\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box B_{i}):1\leq i\leq m\}\quad\text{and}\quad\psi^{\prime}:=\bigwedge\{\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i})):1\leq i\leq m\}.

Note that the valuations in
\mathcal{K}^{1}
and
\mathcal{K}^{2}
are
defined such that
\mathcal{K}^{1}\Vdash\psi
and
\mathcal{K}^{2}\Vdash\psi^{\prime}
.

Section

5.1
implies that

\mathcal{K}^{2}
is also a good
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic and

section

5.1
implies
\mathcal{K}^{2},w_{0}\nVdash\gamma(A)
.
Thus
\mathfrak{d}(\gamma(A))<\mathfrak{d}(A)
.
∎

The rest of this subsection is devoted to prove some technical lemmas required in the proof of
theorem

5.4
.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}
indicates the set of all propositions
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

such that
p_{i}\not\in{\sf sub}(B)
for every
p_{i}\in{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern
