0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}}
. Also let

\Delta_{1}\equiv_{\Gamma}\Delta_{2}
defined as follows:

\forall\,E\in\Gamma\ (\Delta_{1}\vdash E\Leftrightarrow\Delta_{2}\vdash E).

Remember from
theorem

4.6
that
\Phi^{i}_{w}:=\{\varphi^{i}_{w}\}\cup{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{i}_{w}
for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

For every
w\not\sqsubset{w_{1}}
and
E\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}
we have

\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash E
iff
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash E
.
Moreover
for
\sqsubset
-accessible
w
with
w\not\sqsubseteq{w_{1}}

we have
\Phi^{2}_{w}\equiv_{\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}}\Phi^{1}_{w}
and

\Phi^{2}_{w_{1}}\cup\{\chi\}\equiv_{\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}}\Phi^{1}_{w_{1}}
.

Proof.

The second statement is a direct consequence of first one.
So we prove by a double induction, first on

W
ordered by
\sqsupset
and second on
the complexity of
E\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}
and show that

\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash E
iff
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash E
.
So as first induction hypothesis for every
u\sqsupset w

and every
E\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}
we have
\mathcal{K}^{1},u\Vdash E

iff
\mathcal{K}^{2},u\Vdash E
(and hence
\Phi^{2}_{u}\equiv_{\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}}\Phi^{1}_{u}
). Also as second induction
hypothesis assume that for every strict subformula
F
of
E

we have
\mathcal{K}^{1},u\Vdash F
iff
\mathcal{K}^{2},u\Vdash F

for every
u\not\sqsubset w_{1}
.
We have following cases for
E
:

•

E\in{\sf atom}\setminus{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}}
or
E=\bot
.
Obvious.

•

E
is conjunction, disjunction or implication.
All of these cases are easy consequences of
second induction hypothesis.

•

E=\Box F
. Note that by the first induction
hypothesis and definitions of
\Phi^{1}_{u}
and

\Phi^{2}_{u}
we have
\Phi^{1}_{u}\equiv_{\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}}\Phi^{2}_{u}

for every
u\sqsupset w
.
Then by definition of validity
for
\Box F
in preservativity
semantics we have desired result.
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

\mathcal{K}^{2}
is a good
({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf C}{\downarrow}{{\sf S}}{{{\sf N}(\Box)}},{\sf iGL})
-semantic.

Proof.

Section

5.1
implies that
\mathcal{K}^{2}

satisfies compatibility condition. Other required
properties inherited from
\mathcal{K}^{1}
.

Lemma 5.7
.

\psi^{\prime}\vdash B\leftrightarrow\tau(B)
for every

B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
.

Proof.

Easy induction on the complexity of
B
and left to reader.

Lemma 5.8
.

\psi,\psi^{\prime},\chi,q\vdash\gamma(B)\leftrightarrow B
and

\psi^{\prime},\neg q\vdash\gamma(B)\leftrightarrow B
.

Proof.

We only treat first statement for the case
B=a\in{\sf atom}

and leave the rest to reader.
By definition of
\beta
we have

q\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\end{picture}}\ \beta(a)\leftrightarrow\alpha(a)
. On the other hand,

Y^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\end{picture}}\ \alpha(a)\leftrightarrow a
and hence

Y^{\prime},q,\chi^{\prime}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\end{picture}}\ \beta(a)\leftrightarrow a
.
Then
section

5.1
implies

\psi^{\prime},Y^{\prime},q,\chi^{\prime}\vdash\tau\beta(a)\leftrightarrow a
.
Since
\psi
implies that
\eta
is identity,
we get
\psi,\psi^{\prime},Y,q,\chi\vdash\gamma(a)\leftrightarrow a
.
Then since
\vdash Y
we have desired result.
∎

Lemma 5.9
.

\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\psi\wedge\psi^{\prime}

for every
\sqsubset
-accessible
w\not\sqsubset{w_{1}}
. Moreover

\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\gamma(B)\leftrightarrow B
for every
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
and

\sqsubset
-accessible
w\not\sqsubseteq{w_{1}}
.

Proof.

First note that
section

5.1
and

\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\psi\wedge\psi^{\prime}
imply

\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\gamma(B)\leftrightarrow B
whenever
w\not\sqsubseteq{w_{1}}
.
So we only prove the first statement by induction on
w

ordered by
\sqsupset
.
As induction hypothesis
assume that for every
u\sqsupset w
we have

\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\psi\wedge\psi^{\prime}
and hence

\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\gamma(B)\leftrightarrow B
for every
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
,
seeking to show
\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\psi\wedge\psi^{\prime}
.
Since
\psi\wedge\psi^{\prime}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
, it is enough to show

\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\psi\wedge\psi^{\prime}
. So it is enough to show that

\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box B_{i})\wedge\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i}))
. By induction hypothesis
we have
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\Box(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box B_{i})\wedge\Box(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i}))
. Also by definition of

\mathrel{V}^{\prime}
we have
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i})
.
Also by induction hypothesis we have
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\Box(B_{i}\leftrightarrow\gamma(B_{i}))
and hence
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\Box B_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i})
. Thus
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box B_{i}
.
∎

Lemma 5.11
.

\vdash\gamma(B)\leftrightarrow\gamma(\eta(B))

for every
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
.

Proof.

Use induction on the complexity of
B
.
We only treat the case
B=p_{i}

here and leave other cases to reader.
Note that
\gamma:=\tau\circ\beta
and since
\beta

is identity on
p_{i}
we have

\gamma(p_{i})=\tau(p_{i})
. On the other hand, since
\tau

is simultanous fixed-point of

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle p\hfil

we have

\vdash\tau(p_{i})\leftrightarrow\tau\beta(\Box B_{i})
, which implies
\vdash\gamma(p_{i})\leftrightarrow\gamma(\eta(p_{i}))
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

For every
\sqsubset
-accessible
w\in W
we have

\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\gamma(\varphi^{0}_{w})
.

Proof.

We have following cases:

•

w\sqsubset{w_{1}}
: Since
{w_{1}}
is
\sqsubset
-minimal among
the
{\sf H}
-nodes, in this case we have
\varphi^{0}_{w}=\top
and
hence we are trivially done.

•

{w_{1}}\sqsubset w
:
In this case we have
\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\varphi^{0}_{w}
and hence
section

5.1

implies
\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\gamma(\varphi^{0}_{w})
.

•

w={w_{1}}
:
By definition of
\beta
we have

q\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\end{picture}}\ \beta(x)\leftrightarrow\alpha(x)
. On the other hand,

Y^{\prime},\chi^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\end{picture}}\ \alpha(\chi^{\prime})
and hence

Y^{\prime},q,\chi^{\ddagger}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\end{picture}}\ \beta(\chi^{\prime})
.
Then
section

5.1
implies

\psi^{\prime},Y^{\prime},q,\chi^{\ddagger}\vdash\tau\beta(\chi^{\prime})
.
Since
\psi
implies that
\eta
is identity,
we get
\psi,\psi^{\prime},Y,q,\chi^{\dagger}\vdash\gamma(\chi^{\prime})
.
Then
section

5.1
implies

\psi,\psi^{\prime},Y,q,\chi^{\dagger}\vdash\gamma(\chi)
.
Also since
\vdash Y
and

\Phi^{2}_{w_{1}}\vdash q
, we get

\psi,\psi^{\prime},\chi^{\dagger}\vdash\gamma(\chi)
.
On the other hand
section

5.1
implies

\Phi^{2}_{w_{1}}\vdash\psi,\psi^{\prime}
.
Moreover we have
\mathcal{K}^{1},{w_{1}}\Vdash\chi^{\dagger}

(because
\mathcal{K}^{1},{w_{1}}\Vdash\chi
and
{\sf iGL}\vdash\chi\to\chi^{\dagger}
)
and hence by
section

5.1
we have
\mathcal{K}^{2},{w_{1}}\Vdash\chi^{\dagger}
. Since
\chi^{\dagger}\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}

by definition of
\Phi^{2}_{w}

we get
\chi^{\dagger}\in\Phi^{2}_{w_{1}}
.
Hence we may conclude

\Phi^{2}_{w_{1}}\vdash\gamma(\chi)
.

•

Otherwise:
By
section

5.1
we have
\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\psi^{\prime}
.
Then since

\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}\neg q\in\Phi^{2}_{w}
and
\Phi^{2}_{w}\vdash\varphi^{0}_{w}
,

section

5.1
implies desired result.∎

Let us define
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
as the set of all
propositions
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
such that for every
\Box B\in{\sf sub}(A)

we have either
B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
or
q\not\in{\sf sub_{o}}(B)
.
In other words,
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
includes all propositions in which
there is no occurrence of variables besides
q
other than those which occur outside of boxes.

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
Z\subseteq{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
satisfies
“
Z\vdash E
and
E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
implies
E\in Z
”.
Then
C\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
and

Z\vdash C
implies

\vdash E\to C
for some
E\in Z\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
.
Also

Z\vdash\gamma(C)
implies

\vdash\gamma(E)\to\gamma(C)
for some
E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}

with
\gamma(E)\in Z
.

Proof.

First assume that
Z\vdash C
seeking some
E\in Z\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}

such that
\vdash E\to C
. From
Z\vdash C
we get some

F\in Z
such that
\vdash F\to C
. Since
F\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
,
by definition of
\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
(we use
\lfloor{\ }\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
for

\lfloor{\ }\rfloor^{{}^{\!{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}}_{{}_{\!{{\sf iGL}}}}
in the rest of this proof) we get

\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in Z
.
Theorem

3.55
.3 implies
that for every
\Box D\in{\sf sub}(\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}})

either
\Box D\in{\sf sub}(C)
or
\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}D\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
.
Hence by definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
, from
C\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
we get

\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
. Thus
E:=\lfloor{C}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
satisfies all required conditions.

Next assume that
Z\vdash\gamma(C)
seeking some

E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
with
\gamma(E)\in Z
and

\vdash\gamma(E\to C)
.
From
Z\vdash\gamma(C)
we get
some
F\in Z
such that

\vdash F\to\gamma(C)
. Since
F\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
,

sections

3.7
and
3.57
imply

\vdash F\to\gamma(C)^{\star}
,

\vdash\gamma(C)^{\star}\to\gamma(C)
and

{\sf sub^{{\sf B}}_{o}}(\gamma(C)^{\star})\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf B}}_{o}}(\gamma(C))
.
Consider some
\Box D_{0}\in{\sf sub^{{\sf B}}_{o}}(\gamma(C)^{\star})
.
Either we have
\Box D_{0}=\gamma(\Box D)
for some

\Box D\in{\sf sub^{{\sf B}}_{o}}(C)
or

\Box D_{0}\in{\sf sub^{{\sf B}}_{o}}(\gamma(x))
for some
x\in{\sf sub_{o}}(C)
.
In the latter case there exists some
p_{i}\in{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}}
such that

\Box D_{0}=\tau(p_{i})
.
Since
\tau
is the simultaneous fixed point,
we have
\vdash\Box D_{0}\leftrightarrow\gamma(\Box B_{i})
. So we may conclude that
there is some
E_{0}\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\vdash\gamma(C)^{\star}\leftrightarrow\gamma(E_{0})
and by

\vdash\gamma(C)^{\star}\to\gamma(C)
we have

\vdash\gamma(E_{0})\to\gamma(C)
.
On the other hand since

\vdash F\to\gamma(E_{0})
and
F\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
, by

theorem

2.5

we have
\vdash F\to\gamma(E_{0})^{\Box}
. Let
E:=E_{0}^{\Box}
.
Then we have
\gamma(E)\in Z
and
E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
\vdash\gamma(E_{0})\to\gamma(C)
and

\vdash\gamma(E_{0})^{\Box}\to\gamma(E_{0})
, we get

\vdash\gamma(E_{0})^{\Box}\to\gamma(C)
. Since

\gamma(E)=\gamma(E_{0})^{\Box}
we have

\vdash\gamma(E)\to\gamma(C)
. ∎

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
such that for every

\Box E\in{\sf sub_{o}}(B)\cup\{\Box B_{i}:i\leq m\}
and
u\sqsupset w

we have
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash E
iff
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash\gamma(E)
.
Then
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash B\leftrightarrow\gamma(B)
.

Proof.

We use induction on the complexity of
B
. All cases are easy except for:

•

B=p_{i}
for some
i\leq m
.
First note that by assumption of this lemma for every

u\sqsupseteq w
we have

\mathcal{K}^{1},u\Vdash\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(\Box B_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i}))
.
In the other hand by definition of
\mathcal{K}^{1}

we have
\mathcal{K}^{1}\Vdash\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box B_{i})
.
Hence
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i}))
.
Since
\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i}))\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}

we get
\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(p_{i}\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i}))\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{1}_{w}
.
Also by definition of
\gamma
we have

\gamma(p_{i})=\tau(p_{i})
and then since
\tau

is fixed-point we have
\vdash\tau(p_{i})\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i})
.
Thus
\vdash\gamma(p_{i})\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(B_{i})
which implies

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash p_{i}\leftrightarrow\gamma(p_{i})
.

•

B
is a parameter other than
p_{i}
’s or
B=\bot
. Then
\gamma(B)=B
and we are trivially done.

•

B
is a conjunction, disjunction or implication. Easy by induction hypothesis and left to the reader.

•

B=\Box C
. Then by assumption of lemma
for every
u\sqsupseteq w
we have

\mathcal{K}^{1},u\Vdash\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(\Box C\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(C))
. Since

\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(\Box C\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(C))\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
we get

\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(\Box C\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(C))\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{1}_{w}
and thus

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\Box C\leftrightarrow\Box\gamma(C)
.
∎

Section

5.1
states that for every
\sqsubset
-accessible
w\not\sqsubset{w_{1}}
and
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}

we have
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\gamma(B)\leftrightarrow B
.
The following lemma extends a weakening of this result for

w\sqsubset{w_{1}}
.

Lemma 5.15
.

For every
\sqsubset
-accessible
w\in W
and
\Box B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}

we have

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash B
iff

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\gamma(B)
. Moreover
for every
B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})

we have
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash B\leftrightarrow\gamma(B)
.

Proof.

The second statement is a consequence of first statement and
and
section

5.1
.
We use induction on
w
ordered by
\sqsupset
and prove the first statement.
So as induction hypothesis assume that for every

\Box B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
and
u\sqsupset w

we have
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash B
iff
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash\gamma(B)
.
Observe that induction hypothesis together with
section

5.1

imply
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash E\leftrightarrow\gamma(E)
for very
E\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
.
If
w\not\sqsubset{w_{1}}
then
section

5.1

implies desired result.
Since
\Box B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
either we have
B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
or

q\not\in{\sf sub_{o}}(B)
. If
B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
then by induction hypothesis
we have
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash B\leftrightarrow\gamma(B)
which implies the desired result.
So we assume that
w\sqsubset{w_{1}}
and
q\not\in{\sf sub_{o}}(B)
.

•

Assume that
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash B
seeking to show

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\gamma(B)
.
By
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash B
and
section

5.1
we get
some
E\in\Phi^{1}_{w}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\vdash E\to B
.
Since
\Phi^{1}_{w}\subseteq{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})
,
induction hypothesis implies

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\gamma(E)\leftrightarrow E
. In the other hand
from
\vdash E\to B
we get

\vdash\gamma(E)\to\gamma(B)

and thus
\vdash E\to\gamma(B)
.
Since
E\in\Phi^{1}_{w}
we have
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\gamma(B)
.

•

Assume that
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\gamma(B)
seeking to show

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash B
. By induction hypothesis we have

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\gamma(B)\leftrightarrow\hat{\gamma}(B)
and hence

\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\hat{\gamma}(B)
. Let
\lambda
defined as follows:

\lambda(q):=\bot
and
\lambda
is identity elsewhere.
Since
{\sf iGL}
is closed under outer substitutions,
from
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\hat{\gamma}(B)
we get

\hat{\lambda}(\Phi^{1}_{w})\vdash\hat{\lambda}(\hat{\gamma}(B))
. Observe that
by definition of
\gamma
we have

{\sf IPC}\vdash\hat{\lambda}(\hat{\gamma}(B))\leftrightarrow\hat{\lambda}(B)
and since

q\not\in{\sf sub_{o}}(B)
we get

{\sf IPC}\vdash\hat{\lambda}(\hat{\gamma}(B))\leftrightarrow B
.
Hence
\hat{\lambda}(\Phi^{1}_{w})\vdash B
. Obviously
for every
E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
we have
\hat{\lambda}(E)\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
.
Also since
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash\neg q
and

\neg q\vdash E\leftrightarrow\hat{\lambda}(E)
we get
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash\hat{\lambda}(\Phi^{1}_{w})

and thus
\Phi^{1}_{w}\vdash B
.
∎

Lemma 5.16
.

\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash D
iff
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\gamma(D)

for every
D\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
and
w\in W
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{K}^{1}\Vdash\psi
we have
\mathcal{K}^{1}\Vdash\eta(D)\leftrightarrow D
.
Also
section

5.1

implies
\mathcal{K}^{2}\Vdash\gamma(\eta(D))\leftrightarrow\gamma(D)
.
Since for every
1\leq i\leq m
we have
p_{i}\not\in{\sf sub}(\eta(D))

and
D\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
implies
\eta(D)\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
,
it is enough to prove the statement of lemma with the extra assumption that
D\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}
.

First by induction on
w\in W^{\prime}
, ordered by
\sqsupset
,
we show that for every
D\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
,
we have
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash D
iff
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\gamma(D)
.
We use a second induction on the complexity of
D

and prove the statement of this lemma.
So by first induction hypothesis we have

\mathcal{K}^{1},u\Vdash C
iff

\mathcal{K}^{2},u\Vdash\gamma(C)
for every

C\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
and
u\sqsupset w
.
Also as second induction hypothesis we have

\mathcal{K}^{1},w^{\prime}\Vdash C
iff

\mathcal{K}^{2},w^{\prime}\Vdash\gamma(C)

for every
w^{\prime}\in W
and

C
which is a strict sub-formula of
D
.
We have following cases for
D
:

•

D=\bot
or
D
is a parameter: Since
D\neq p_{i}

by definition of
\gamma
we have
\gamma(D)=D
. Then obviously
by definitions of
\mathcal{K}^{1}
and
\mathcal{K}^{2}

we have desired result.

•

D
is conjunction,
disjunction or implication:
straightforward by second induction hypothesis.

•

D=\Box C
.

–

First assume that
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash\Box C
and
consider some

u\sqsupset w
seeking to show
\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
. By definition of
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash\Box C
we have

\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash C
.
We have three cases:
(1)
u\not\sqsubseteq{w_{1}}
.
Then
section

5.1
implies
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
and hence

section

5.1
implies
\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
.
(2)
u={w_{1}}
. Then
{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}_{u}\vdash\varphi^{0}_{u}\to C
. Moreover
since
q\not\in{\sf sub}(\varphi^{0}_{u})
we have
\varphi^{0}_{u}\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence
\varphi^{0}_{u}\to C\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
and
section

5.1
implies

\vdash E\to(\varphi^{0}_{u}\to C)
for some

E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}_{u}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
. Then

\gamma(E),\gamma(\varphi^{0}_{u})\vdash\gamma(C)
. Since

E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
, we get
\mathcal{K}^{1},u\Vdash E

and first induction hypothesis implies
\mathcal{K}^{2},u\Vdash\gamma(E)
and thus
\gamma(E)\in\Phi^{2}_{u}
.
Also
section

5.1
implies
\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\gamma(\varphi^{0}_{u})

and hence
\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
.
(3)
u\sqsubset{w_{1}}
. Then
\Phi^{1}_{u}\subseteq{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}

and
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash C
together with
section

5.1

implies
\vdash E\to C
for some
E\in\Phi^{1}_{u}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
.
Thus
\vdash\gamma(E)\to\gamma(C)
.
Since
E\in\Phi^{1}_{u}
we have
\mathcal{K}^{1},u\Vdash E
and first
induction hypothesis implies
\mathcal{K}^{2},u\Vdash\gamma(E)
.
Since
\gamma(E)\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
we get
\gamma(E)\in\Phi^{2}_{u}

and thus
\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
.

–

For the other direction,
let
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\gamma(\Box C)

and
u\sqsupset w

seeking to show
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash C
.
By definition of
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\Box\gamma(C)
we get
\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
.
First assume that
u\not\sqsubset{w_{1}}
.
Then
\Phi^{2}_{u},\varphi^{0}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
and hence

section

5.1
implies

\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
. Then
section

5.1
implies

\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash C
.

Next assume that
u\sqsubset{w_{1}}
.
From
\Phi^{2}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
and
section

5.1

there is some
E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}

such that
\gamma(E)\in\Phi^{2}_{u}
and

\vdash\gamma(E\to C)
.
Hence
\mathcal{K}^{2},u\Vdash\gamma(E)

and by first induction hypothesis we get
\mathcal{K}^{1},u\Vdash E
.
Since
E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
we have
E\in\Phi^{1}_{u}
. On the other hand

\vdash\gamma(E\to C)
implies
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash\gamma(E\to C)

and hence
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash\gamma(E)\to\gamma(C)
. Since

E\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf parb})\cap\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}

section

5.1
implies
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash E\leftrightarrow\gamma(E)
and then
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash\gamma(C)
.
Thus
section

5.1
implies
\Phi^{1}_{u}\vdash C
.
∎

Corollary 5.17
.

\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash D
iff
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\gamma(D)

for every
D\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}^{\prime\prime}
and
w\succcurlyeq w_{0}
.

Proof.

We use induction on the complexity of
D
:

•

D\in{\sf parb}
:
Section

5.1
.

•

D
is atomic variable:
Since
w\succcurlyeq w_{0}
we have
w\neq{w_{1}}
and hence

\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\neg q
and by
section

5.1

we have
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash\gamma(x)\leftrightarrow x
.
Then since
\mathcal{K}^{2},w\Vdash x
iff
\mathcal{K}^{1},w\Vdash x
,
we have desired result.

•

D
is conjunction, disjunction or implication:
Easy by induction hypothesis and left to reader.
∎

5.2
Second step reduction:
{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash A
implies

{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\nvdash\beta(A)

In this section we show that
{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash A
implies
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\nvdash\beta(A)
for some substitution
\beta
.
The idea behind this reduction is some uniform collection of finitely many reductions of
{\sf iGL}
to
{\sf iGLC_{a}}
.

For simplicity of notations,
in this subsection we use
\lfloor{\ }\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}

instead of
\lfloor{\ }\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf iGL}}}_{{}_{\!{{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}}}}
.
Also we fix some
A\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
and define:

\Delta:=\{E\in{\sf sub}(A):E\text{ is atomic or boxed}\},\quad\Delta^{\prime}:=\{E\in{\sf sub}(A):E\text{ is parameter or boxed}\}

Y:=\mathcal{L}_{0}(\Delta),\quad Y^{\prime}:={\sf NNIL}(\Delta^{\prime}),\quad Y^{\prime\prime}:=\{E^{\Box}:E\in Y^{\prime}\}.

Note that all of the above mentioned sets of propositions
except for
Y
are finite modulo
{\sf iK4}
-provable equivalence.
Moreover
sections

3.7
and
3.57
imply that
for every
E\in Y
we have
\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in Y^{\prime\prime}
.

Theorem 5.18
.

If
{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash A
,
then there is some substitution
\beta

such that
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\nvdash\beta(A)
.

Proof.

Let
{\sf iGL}{{\sf H}^{\Box}}\nvdash A
. Then
section

4.1
implies that there is some good

({{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)},{\sf iGL})
-semantic
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\mathrel{V},\varphi)

with the root
w_{0}
such that

\mathcal{K},w_{0}\nVdash A
. Moreover we assume that for every atomic

a\not\in{\sf sub}(A)
and
w\in W
we have
\mathcal{K},w\nVdash a
.
Let
S:=\{E:\Box E\in{\sf sub}(A)\}
and

X^{A}_{w}:=\Phi_{w}\cap Y^{\prime\prime}
, in which
Y^{\prime\prime}
is as
defined just before this lemma.
Note that for every
E\in S

we have
\Phi_{w}\vdash E
iff

\Phi_{w}\vdash\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}
iff
\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in X^{A}_{w}
. Hence

\Phi_{w}\vdash E
iff
X^{A}_{w}\vdash E
for
E\in S
.
Then for every

w\in W
and
E\in S
with
\Phi_{w}\nvdash E
let

\mathcal{K}^{w}_{E}:=(W^{w}_{E},\preccurlyeq^{w}_{E},\sqsubset^{w}_{E},\mathrel{V}^{w}_{E})

be some good Kripke model with the root
w_{E}
such that

\mathcal{K}^{w}_{E},w_{E}\Vdash X^{A}_{w}
and
\mathcal{K}^{w}_{E},w_{E}\nVdash E
,
as provided by
theorem

2.2
.
Moreover assume that if
(w,E)\neq(u,F)
then

W^{w}_{E}
and
W^{u}_{F}
and
W
are disjoint.
Also define

\widehat{W}:=W\cup\bigcup_{E\in S,\,w\in W}W^{E}_{w}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\sqsubset}:={\sqsubset}\cup\bigcup_{\Phi_{w}\nvdash E}{\sqsubset^{w}_{E}}.

Also for every
w\in\widehat{W}
let
\widehat{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash B
indicates

•

if
w\in W
then
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
,

•

if
w\in W^{u}_{E}
then
\mathcal{K}^{u}_{E},w\Vdash B
.

Let
\{q_{w}:w\in\widehat{W}\}
be a fresh set of pairwise distinct
atomic parameters, i.e.
q_{u}\neq q_{v}

whenever
u\neq v
and
q_{u}\not\in{\sf sub}(A)
for every
u\in\widehat{W}
.
Then define the substitution
\beta
as follows:

\beta(x):=\bigvee\{Q_{w}:w\in\widehat{W}\text{ and }\widehat{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash x\}\quad\text{ and }\quad Q_{w}:=q_{w}\wedge\bigwedge_{w\widehat{\sqsubset}u}\neg q_{u}.

Also define the preservativity semantic

\bar{\mathcal{K}}:=(W,\preccurlyeq,\sqsubset,\bar{\mathrel{V}},\bar{X})
for
({\sf S}{\sf N},{\sf iGLC_{a}})
as follows:

•

\bar{\mathrel{V}}
is defined such that
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\nVdash x

for every atomic
variable
x
and
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash q_{u}
iff
u\mathrel{\widetilde{\sqsubseteq}}w
iff

\exists v\,(u\sqsubseteq v\preccurlyeq w)
.
Moreover
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash p
iff
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash p
for every other parameter
p
. Note that by this definition
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\nVdash q_{u}

for every
w\in W
and
u\in\widehat{W}\setminus W
.

•

\bar{X}_{w}:={{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}^{\prime}_{w}:=\{E\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}:\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash E\}
.

Similarly define

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{w}_{E}:=(W^{w}_{E},\preccurlyeq^{w}_{E},\sqsubset^{w}_{E},\bar{V}^{w}_{E})
in which

\bar{V}^{w}_{E}
is defined in this way:

u\mathrel{\bar{\mathrel{V}}^{w}_{E}}a
iff either

•

a=q_{v}
such that
u
is accessible from
v
via the transitive closure of

\preccurlyeq^{w}_{E}\cup\sqsubset^{w}_{E}
or

•

a\in{\sf sub}(A)
is a parameter and
u\mathrel{V}^{w}_{E}a
.

Section

5.2
implies
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\nVdash\beta(A)
and hence
by
section

4.1
we have
{\sf iGLC_{a}}{{\sf H}_{\sigma}}\nvdash\beta(A)
.
∎

Lemma 5.19
.

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
iff
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\beta(B)
for every

w\in W
and
B\in Y
.

Proof.

We prove an strengthen of this lemma for arbitrary

B\in Z:=Y\cup Y^{\prime\prime}
instead of
B\in Y
.
We use double induction, first on
w\in W
ordered by
\sqsupset

and second on
B\in Z
. So as first induction hypothesis
we have
\mathcal{K},w^{\prime}\Vdash B^{\prime}
iff
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w^{\prime}\Vdash\beta(B^{\prime})

for every
w^{\prime}\sqsupset w
and every
B^{\prime}\in Z
.
Also as second induction hypothesis we have

\mathcal{K},w^{\prime}\Vdash B^{\prime}
iff
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w^{\prime}\Vdash\beta(B^{\prime})

for every
w^{\prime}\succcurlyeq w
and
B^{\prime}
a strict subformula of
B
.
We have the following cases for
B\in Z
:

•

B
is a parameter. Since
B\in Z
we have

B\neq q_{w}
for every
w\in\widehat{W}
.
Then since
\beta(p)=p
for every parameter
p
,
by definition of
\bar{\mathrel{V}}
in

\bar{\mathcal{K}}
we have desired result.

•

B=x
is an atomic variable.
Section

5.2
.

•

B
is conjunction, disjunction or implication:
easy by first induction hypothesis and left to the reader.

•

B=\Box E
.
First assume that

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\Box E
and
u\sqsupset w
seeking to show

\bar{X}_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGLC_{a}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \beta(E)
.
From
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\Box E

we get
\Phi_{u}\vdash E
and hence

\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\in\Phi_{u}\cap Y^{\prime\prime}
. This implies

\mathcal{K},u\Vdash\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}

and first induction hypothesis implies

\bar{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash\beta(\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}})

and thus
\beta(\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}})\in\bar{\Phi}_{u}
.
Since
\vdash\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}}\to E
we have
\vdash\beta(\lfloor{E}\rfloor^{{}^{\!}}_{{}_{\!{}}})\to\beta(E)
and thus
\bar{X}_{u}\vdash\beta(E)

and a fortiori
\bar{X}_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGLC_{a}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \beta(E)
.

For the other direction,
let
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\Box\beta(E)

and
u\sqsupset w
seeking to show
\Phi_{u}\vdash E
.
From
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\Box\beta(E)
we get

\bar{X}_{u}\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGLC_{a}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \beta(E)
and hence

\bar{X}_{u},\bigwedge_{x}\,\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\Box$}\put(0.5,0.65){.}\end{picture}(x\to\Box x)\vdash\beta(E)
.
Since
\Box(x\to\Box x)\in\bar{X}_{u}
, we have

\bar{X}_{u},\bigwedge_{x}(x\to\Box x)\vdash\beta(E)
.
Since
{\sf iGL}
is closed under outer substitutions,
we have
\hat{\theta}(\bar{X}_{u}),\hat{\theta}(\bigwedge_{x}(x\to\Box x))\vdash\hat{\theta}\beta(E)
in which
\theta
is the substitution which replaces

\bot
for every variable
x
. Thus

\bar{X}_{u}\vdash\beta(E)

and then there is some

F\in\bar{X}_{u}
such that
\vdash F\to\beta(E)
.
Hence
section

5.2
implies
\vdash F\to\beta(G)
and

\vdash\beta(G)\to\beta(E)
for some
G\in Y^{\prime\prime}
.
Thus
\beta(G)\in\bar{X}_{u}
and then
\bar{\mathcal{K}},u\Vdash\beta(G)
.
Hence by second induction hypothesis
we have
\mathcal{K},u\Vdash G

and
G\in X^{A}_{u}
. Then
\beta(X^{A}_{u})\vdash\beta(E)
and thus

section

5.2
implies
X^{A}_{u}\vdash E
and
then
\Phi_{u}\vdash E
. ∎

Lemma 5.20
.

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash x
iff
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\beta(x)
for every

w\in W
and
x\in{\sf var}
. Also
we have
\mathcal{K}^{w}_{E},u\Vdash x
iff

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{w}_{E},u\Vdash\beta(x)
for every
u\in W^{w}_{E}
and
x\in{\sf var}
.

Proof.

The proof of second statement is similar to the first
one and left to reader.
For the first statement we have following argument.
First assume that

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash x
. It is enough to show
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash Q_{w}
.
Obviously by definition of
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
we have
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash q_{w}
.
Let
u\in\widehat{W}
such that
w\mathrel{\widehat{\sqsubset}}u

seeking to show
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\neg q_{u}
.
Hence
u\in W
and
w\sqsubset u
. For every
w^{\prime}\succcurlyeq w

we have
u\not\mathrel{\widetilde{\sqsubseteq}}w^{\prime}
, lest

u\sqsubseteq v\preccurlyeq w^{\prime}
and
w\preccurlyeq w^{\prime}
implies that
v
and
w
are

\preccurlyeq
-comparable because
(W,\preccurlyeq)
is tree, and hence transcendentality implies
v\sqsubset v
contradicting irreflexivity.
Hence
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w^{\prime}\nVdash q_{u}
and thus
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\neg q_{u}
.

For other direction around, assume that
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\beta(x)

seeking to show
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash x
. From
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\beta(x)

we get some
w^{\prime}\in\widehat{W}
such that
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash Q_{w^{\prime}}

and
\widehat{\mathcal{K}},w^{\prime}\Vdash x
. From

\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash Q_{w^{\prime}}
we have

\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash q_{w^{\prime}}
and hence
w^{\prime}\sqsubseteq v\preccurlyeq w

for some
v\in W
with
\mathcal{K},w^{\prime}\Vdash x
. If
w^{\prime}=v

then
w^{\prime}\preccurlyeq w
and thus
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash x
.
Otherwise we have
w^{\prime}\sqsubset v
and hence
w^{\prime}\mathrel{\widehat{\sqsubset}}v

which implies
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash\neg q_{v}
and thus

\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\nVdash q_{v}
. On the other hand since
v\preccurlyeq w

we have
v\mathrel{\widetilde{\sqsubseteq}}w
and hence
\bar{\mathcal{K}},w\Vdash q_{v}
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 5.21
.

\beta(X^{A}_{w})\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGLC_{a}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \beta(E)
implies
X^{A}_{w}\vdash E

for every
\Box E\in{\sf sub}(A)
.

Proof.

We reason contrapositively. Assume that

X^{A}_{w}\nvdash E
seeking to show

\beta(X^{A}_{w})\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGLC_{a}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \beta(E)
.
From
X^{A}_{w}\nvdash E
we have
\Phi_{w}\nvdash E
and hence

\mathcal{K}^{w}_{E},w_{E}\Vdash X^{A}_{w}
and
\mathcal{K}^{w}_{E},w_{E}\nVdash E
.

Section

5.2
imples

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{w}_{E},w_{E}\Vdash\beta(X^{A}_{w})
and

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{w}_{E},w_{E}\nVdash E
and thus

\beta(X^{A}_{w})\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\nvdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf iGLC_{a}}}$}\end{picture}}\ \ \beta(E)
.
∎

Lemma 5.22
.

For every
u\in W^{w}_{E}
and

B\in\mathcal{L}_{\Box}
with
{\sf sub^{{\sf par}}}(B)\subseteq{\sf sub^{{\sf par}}}(A)

we have
\mathcal{K}^{w}_{E},u\Vdash B
iff

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{w}_{E},u\Vdash\beta(B)
.

Proof.

We prove this lemma by induction on complexity of
B
. All
inductive steps are derived easily by induction hypothesis, except for basic steps:

•

B=x
is an atomic variable.

Section

5.2
.

•

B=p
is a parameter.
By assumption of this lemma we must have
p\in{\sf sub}(A)
and hence by definition of
\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{w}_{E}
we have

\mathcal{K}^{w}_{E},u\Vdash p
iff

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{w}_{E},u\Vdash p
.
∎

Lemma 5.23
.

Let
\vdash F\to\beta(E)
for some
F\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
and
E\in Y\cup Y^{\prime\prime}
.
Then there is some
G\in Y^{\prime\prime}
such that

\vdash F\to\beta(G)
and
\vdash\beta(G)\to\beta(E)
.

Proof.

If
E\in Y^{\prime\prime}
we are trivially done by
G:=E
.
So assume that
E\in Y
.
Assume that
\vdash F\to\beta(E)
seeking some

G\in Y^{\prime\prime}
with
\vdash F\to\beta(G)

and
\vdash\beta(G\to E)
.
Since
F\in{{\sf S}{\sf N}(\Box)}
,
sections

3.7
and
3.57

imply
\vdash(\beta(E))^{\star}\to\beta(E)
and

\vdash F\to(\beta(E))^{\star}
with

(\beta(E))^{\star}\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}
and

{\sf sub_{o}}((\beta(E))^{\star})\subseteq{\sf sub_{o}}(\beta(E))
.
Hence there is some
G_{0}\in{{\sf N}(\Box)}
such that

\beta(G_{0})=(\beta(E))^{\star}
and

{\sf sub_{o}}(G_{0})\subseteq{\sf sub_{o}}(E)
and thus
G_{0}\in Y
.
Let
G:=G_{0}^{\Box}
.
Obviously we have
G\in Y^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
\vdash F\to\beta(G_{0})
,
theorem

2.5

implies
\vdash F\to\beta(G)
. Moreover since

\vdash\beta(G)\to\beta(G_{0})
and
\vdash\beta(G_{0})\to\beta(E)

we get
\vdash\beta(G\to E)
.
∎
